<path>/source/standards/karpathy-guidelines.mdc</path>
<type>file</type>
<content>
1: ---
2: description: Behavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Use when writing, reviewing, or refactoring code to avoid overcomplication, make surgical changes, surface assumptions, and define verifiable success criteria.
3: alwaysApply: true
4: ---
5: 
6: # Karpathy behavioral guidelines
7: 
8: Behavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Merge with project-specific instructions as needed.
9: 
10: **Tradeoff:** These guidelines bias toward caution over speed. For trivial tasks, use judgment.
11: 
12: ## 1. Think Before Coding
13: 
14: **Don't assume. Don't hide confusion. Surface tradeoffs.**
15: 
16: Before implementing:
17: 
18: - State your assumptions explicitly. If uncertain, ask.
19: - If multiple interpretations exist, present them - don't pick silently.
20: - If a simpler approach exists, say so. Push back when warranted.
21: - If something is unclear, stop. Name what's confusing. Ask.
22: 
23: ## 2. Simplicity First
24: 
25: **Minimum code that solves the problem. Nothing speculative.**
26: 
27: - No features beyond what was asked.
28: - No abstractions for single-use code.
29: - No "flexibility" or "configurability" that wasn't requested.
30: - No error handling for impossible scenarios.
31: - If you write 200 lines and it could be 50, rewrite it.
32: 
33: Ask yourself: "Would a senior engineer say this is overcomplicated?" If yes, simplify.
34: 
35: ## 3. Surgical Changes
36: 
37: **Touch only what you must. Clean up only your own mess.**
38: 
39: When editing existing code:
40: 
41: - Don't "improve" adjacent code, comments, or formatting.
42: - Don't refactor things that aren't broken.
43: - Match existing style, even if you'd do it differently.
44: - If you notice unrelated dead code, mention it - don't delete it.
45: 
46: When your changes create orphans:
47: 
48: - Remove imports/variables/functions that YOUR changes made unused.
49: - Don't remove pre-existing dead code unless asked.
50: 
51: The test: Every changed line should trace directly to the user's request.
52: 
53: ## 4. Goal-Driven Execution
54: 
55: **Define success criteria. Loop until verified.**
56: 
57: Transform tasks into verifiable goals:
58: 
59: - "Add validation" → "Write tests for invalid inputs, then make them pass"
60: - "Fix the bug" → "Write a test that reproduces it, then make it pass"
61: - "Refactor X" → "Ensure tests pass before and after"
62: 
63: For multi-step tasks, state a brief plan:
64: 
65: ```
66: 1. [Step] → verify: [check]
67: 2. [Step] → verify: [check]
68: 3. [Step] → verify: [check]
69: ```
70: 
71: Strong success criteria let you loop independently. Weak criteria ("make it work") require constant clarification.
72: 
73: ---
74: 
75: **These guidelines are working if:** fewer unnecessary changes in diffs, fewer rewrites due to overcomplication, and clarifying questions come before implementation rather than after mistakes.

(End of file - total 75 lines)
</content>

<path>/source/standards/ponytail.mdc</path>
<type>file</type>
<content>
1: ---
2: description: Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode. Always pick the simplest solution that works.
3: globs:
4: alwaysApply: true
5: ---
6: 
7: # Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode
8: 
9: You are a lazy senior developer. Lazy means efficient, not careless. The best code is the code never written.
10: 
11: Before writing any code, stop at the first rung that holds:
12: 
13: 1. Does this need to be built at all? (YAGNI)
14: 2. Does it already exist in this codebase? Reuse the helper, util, or pattern that's already here, don't re-write it.
15: 3. Does the standard library already do this? Use it.
16: 4. Does a native platform feature cover it? Use it.
17: 5. Does an already-installed dependency solve it? Use it.
18: 6. Can this be one line? Make it one line.
19: 7. Only then: write the minimum code that works.
20: 
21: The ladder runs after you understand the problem, not instead of it: read the task and the code it touches, trace the real flow end to end, then climb.
22: 
23: Bug fix = root cause, not symptom: a report names a symptom. Grep every caller of the function you touch and fix the shared function once — one guard there is a smaller diff than one per caller, and patching only the path the ticket names leaves a sibling caller still broken.
24: 
25: Rules:
26: 
27: - No abstractions that weren't explicitly requested.
28: - No new dependency if it can be avoided.
29: - No boilerplate nobody asked for.
30: - Deletion over addition. Boring over clever. Fewest files possible.
31: - Shortest working diff wins, but only once you understand the problem. The smallest change in the wrong place isn't lazy, it's a second bug.
32: - Question complex requests: "Do you actually need X, or does Y cover it?"
33: - Pick the edge-case-correct option when two stdlib approaches are the same size, lazy means less code, not the flimsier algorithm.
34: - Mark deliberate simplifications that cut a real corner with a known ceiling (global lock, O(n²) scan, naive heuristic) with a `ponytail:` comment naming the ceiling and upgrade path.
35: 
36: Not lazy about: understanding the problem (read it fully and trace the real flow before picking a rung, a small diff you don't understand is just laziness dressed up as efficiency), input validation at trust boundaries, error handling that prevents data loss, security, accessibility, the calibration real hardware needs (the platform is never the spec ideal, a clock drifts, a sensor reads off), anything explicitly requested. Lazy code without its check is unfinished: non-trivial logic leaves ONE runnable check behind, the smallest thing that fails if the logic breaks (an assert-based demo/self-check or one small test file; no frameworks, no fixtures). Trivial one-liners need no test.

(End of file - total 36 lines)
</content>

186: diff --git a/docs/manual/kinds/mikrotik_chr.md b/docs/manual/kinds/mikrotik_chr.md

191: @@ -0,0 +1,156 @@
192: +---
193: +search:
194: +  boost: 4
195: +kind_code_name: mikrotik_chr
196: +kind_display_name: MikroTik RouterOS CHR
197: +---
198: +# -{{ kind_display_name }}-
199: +
200: +[MikroTik RouterOS](https://mikrotik.com/software) Cloud Hosted Router (CHR) from the **official [`mikrotik/chr`](https://hub.docker.com/r/mikrotik/chr) image** is identified with the `-{{ kind_code_name }}-` kind in the [topology file](../topo-def-file.md). The image is published by MikroTik for `amd64` and `arm64` and is used as is: no [vrnetlab](../vrnetlab.md) build is needed.
201: +
202: +The image is a QEMU VM (Alpine, qemu and the CHR disk) in a container. Its own `/start.sh` is not made for orchestrated links ([#3116](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/issues/3116)), so the kind writes a launcher script into the node's lab directory, mounts it read-only and runs it as the entrypoint instead. The launcher uses only the tools that ship in the image.
203: +
204: +/// note
205: +The [`mikrotik_ros`](vr-ros.md) kind runs CHR from an image you build with vrnetlab. Both kinds use the same interface naming and startup config format, so a lab moves from one to the other by changing `kind` and `image`.
206: +///
207: +
208: +## Getting -{{ kind_display_name }}- image
209: +
210: +```bash
211: +docker pull mikrotik/chr:7.24.5
212: +```
213: +
214: +Tags follow RouterOS versions, plus `stable`, `long-term`, `testing` and `development`. This kind has been tested with 7.24.5 (stable), 7.23.8 (long-term) and 7.25rc2 (testing).
215: +
216: +## Managing -{{ kind_display_name }}- nodes
217: +
218: +/// tab | CLI
219: +```bash
220: +ssh admin@<node-name>
221: +```
222: +///
223: +/// tab | REST API
224: +```bash
225: +curl -u admin:admin http://<node-name>/rest/system/resource
226: +```
227: +///
228: +/// tab | Winbox / API
229: +Winbox (TCP 8291) and the RouterOS API (TCP 8728, 8729 for TLS) are reachable on the node's management address.
230: +///
231: +/// tab | Console
232: +The serial console is on port 5000 inside the container:
233: +```bash
234: +docker exec -it <node-name> nc 127.0.0.1 5000
235: +```
236: +///
237: +/// tab | bash
238: +```bash
239: +docker exec -it <node-name> sh
240: +```
241: +///
242: +
243: +The node's management address is RouterOS's own `ether1` address ([transparent management](#management-connectivity)), so every service RouterOS runs answers on it directly, with nothing forwarded.
244: +
245: +/// note
246: +Default credentials are `admin:admin`. With custom [`credentials`](../nodes.md#credentials) the kind creates that user in the `full` group and disables the factory `admin` account, which has a blank password on a fresh CHR disk.
247: +///
248: +
249: +## Interface naming
250: +
251: +You can use [interface names](../topo-def-file.md#interface-naming) in the topology file like they appear in RouterOS: `etherX`, where `X` is the port number.
252: +
253: +* `ether2` - first data port
254: +* `ether3` - second data port, and so on
255: +
256: +/// admonition
257: +    type: warning
258: +Data ports start at `ether2`: `ether1` is the management interface. Using `ether1` in a topology results in an error.
259: +///
260: +
261: +The container interface `ethN` is RouterOS `ether(N+1)`, also when links leave gaps: a link on `eth12` alone still lands on `ether13`, with `ether2` to `ether12` present and unlinked. Interface MTU follows the link MTU, and LLDP frames are forwarded, so `/ip neighbor` shows the peers.
262: +
263: +## Node configuration
264: +
265: +-{{ kind_display_name }}- nodes boot with the default CHR configuration plus the credentials, the identity (set to the node name) and the management setup described below.
266: +
267: +### Startup configuration
268: +
269: +A RouterOS script (`.rsc`) set with [`startup-config`](../nodes.md#startup-config) is imported once the router is up:
270: +
271: +```yaml
272: +name: chr_lab
273: +topology:
274: +  nodes:
275: +    r1:
276: +      kind: mikrotik_chr
277: +      image: mikrotik/chr:7.24.5
278: +      startup-config: r1.rsc
279: +```
280: +
281: +The file is copied to the node's lab directory as `ftpboot/config.auto.rsc` and imported over the REST API. RouterOS imports line by line and stops at the first error: in that case the container log carries RouterOS's own reason with the line number, for example
282: +
283: +```
284: +chr-launch: startup config import FAILED: Script Error: bad parameter network-type (line 3 column 59); lines before the failing one were applied, the rest were not
285: +```
286: +
287: +and the node does not become ready.
288: +
289: +### Saving configuration
290: +
291: +[`containerlab save`](../../cmd/save.md) writes each node's `/export` to `ftpboot/config.auto.rsc` in its lab directory. The management objects the launcher creates at every boot (everything tagged `clab-mgmt`, and the `ether1` DHCP client) are left out, so the saved file can be used as the startup config of the next deployment, also one with different management addresses.
292: +
293: +### Node readiness
294: +
295: +The node is ready once RouterOS accepted the credentials and the startup config, reaches its gateway from the management address and, unless the configuration disables SSH, its SSH server answers. An SSH server that does not answer within `CHR_BOOT_WAIT` is logged, and the node is still declared ready, since REST and the console work. The container log then shows
296: +
297: +```
298: +chr-launch: RouterOS ready
299: +```
300: +
301: +and the kind's default [healthcheck](../nodes.md#healthcheck) reports the container `healthy`, so [`stages`](../nodes.md#stages) with `wait-for` can depend on it. A node whose startup config was rejected never becomes healthy. A `healthcheck` set in the topology replaces the default one.
302: +
303: +### Resources
304: +
305: +The kind reads the official image's own environment variables:
306: +
307: +| Variable | Default | Purpose |
308: +|----------|---------|---------|
309: +| `MEM` | 256 MiB + 8 MiB per data interface and vCPU | VM memory (a QEMU `-m` value, e.g. `1024` or `1G`) |
310: +| `CPUS` | `1` | VM vCPUs |
311: +| `DISK` | `128M` | minimum disk size (the disk only grows) |
312: +| `CHR_INTF_WAIT` | `60` | seconds to wait for the data interfaces containerlab attaches before the VM starts |
313: +| `CHR_BOOT_WAIT` | `300` | seconds to wait for RouterOS to answer, for the startup config import, and for SSH before the node is declared ready |
314: +
315: +```yaml
316: +r1:
317: +  kind: mikrotik_chr
318: +  image: mikrotik/chr:7.24.5
319: +  env:
320: +    MEM: 1024
321: +    CPUS: 2
322: +```
323: +
324: +KVM is used when `/dev/kvm` is available. Without it the image's QEMU runs under TCG emulation: the node works, but boots several times slower.
325: +
326: +## Management connectivity
327: +
328: +Management is transparent: the IPv4 and IPv6 addresses and gateways containerlab gives the node's `eth0` are configured on RouterOS `ether1`, and the container's `eth0` is bridged to the VM's `ether1`. The router is the node's management address: SSH, the REST API, Winbox, SNMP and every other service answer on it, LLDP/MNDP work on the management network, and the address in the topology table is the one in the RouterOS configuration.
329: +
330: +The image ships busybox only (no `tc`), so where vrnetlab's [transparent management](../vrnetlab.md#management-interface) redirects with tc-mirred, this kind uses a Linux bridge. The container itself has no address on the management network; it reaches RouterOS for bootstrap over IPv6 link-local on that bridge.
331: +
332: +The management return path lives in its own routing table, `mgmt`, selected by routing rules for the management addresses, and CHR's `ether1` DHCP client is disabled. A default route in the lab configuration, a PPPoE client's default route or a dual-WAN setup in `main` therefore does not cut SSH or REST access to the node. RouterOS's router advertisements on `ether1` carry a zero router lifetime, so no host on the management network takes the router as its IPv6 default gateway; the container joins the management network only once this is in place. The objects carry the comment `clab-mgmt` and are re-applied at boot when the container's addressing changed.
333: +
334: +/// warning
335: +Do not remove or change the `clab-mgmt` objects or the `mgmt` routing table: management of the node depends on them.
336: +///
337: +
338: +## Restarts
339: +
340: +[`containerlab restart`](../../cmd/restart.md) and a `/system reboot` inside RouterOS keep the interface names, MAC addresses, configuration and links: the launcher persists the NIC identity in the container on first boot and does not re-run the bootstrap. A plain `docker restart` keeps the router and its management, but Docker deletes the node's veth links, which stay down until the node is restarted with containerlab or the lab is redeployed.
341: +
342: +## File mounts
343: +
344: +For each node containerlab creates in its [lab directory](../conf-artifacts.md):
345: +
346: +* `ftpboot/` - mounted to `/ftpboot`, holds `config.auto.rsc`
347: +* `chr-launch.sh` - the launcher, mounted read-only to `/chr-launch.sh` and rewritten on every deploy
348: diff --git a/mkdocs.yml b/mkdocs.yml
349: index 914ad86..d6273e0 100644
350: --- a/mkdocs.yml
351: +++ b/mkdocs.yml
352: @@ -46,10 +46,11 @@ nav:
353:            - FTOS10v: manual/kinds/vr-ftosv.md
354:            - Enterprise SONiC: manual/kinds/dell_sonic.md
355:        - PLVision:
356:            - Enterprise SONiC: manual/kinds/plvision_sonic.md
357:        - MikroTik RouterOS: manual/kinds/vr-ros.md
358: +      - MikroTik RouterOS CHR (official image): manual/kinds/mikrotik_chr.md
359:        - Huawei VRP: manual/kinds/huawei_vrp.md
360:        - IPInfusion OcNOS: manual/kinds/ipinfusion-ocnos.md

368: +++ b/nodes/mikrotik_chr/chr-launch.sh

370: +#!/bin/sh
371: +# chr-launch.sh: run the OFFICIAL mikrotik/chr image (MikroTik RouterOS Cloud
372: +# Hosted Router) as a containerlab node, image untouched.
373: +#
374: +# The image is MikroTik's own Docker Hub distribution (Alpine + qemu +
375: +# /diskimage/chr-<ver>.img + /start.sh). This script is bind-mounted into the
376: +# node and set as its entrypoint by the containerlab `mikrotik_chr` kind. It replaces
377: +# the image's /start.sh, which is not orchestration-ready (containerlab#3116):
378: +# it scans interfaces once before containerlab attaches the links, bridges the
379: +# management NIC so the container IP never reaches the router, and imports no
380: +# startup config.
381: +#
382: +# What it does (vrnetlab's mikrotik/routeros launch.py is the model, re-done
383: +# with the image's own busybox + qemu: no python, no ftp client, no tc):
384: +#   1. waits for the CLAB_INTFS data interfaces containerlab attaches (eth1..N)
385: +#   2. management, transparent: the container's eth0 is bridged with the VM's
386: +#      ether1 tap, and eth0's addresses and default gateways move into RouterOS
387: +#      ether1. The router IS the node's management address: every service and
388: +#      LLDP reach it directly, nothing is port-forwarded. The image ships only
389: +#      busybox (no tc), so this uses a bridge where vrnetlab uses tc-mirred
390: +#   3. data: one tap per container interface, bridged pairwise (ethN <-> tapN,
391: +#      LLDP forwarded). NIC order in the VM follows the eth index, so container
392: +#      ethN is always RouterOS ether(N+1), even with gaps
393: +#   4. after boot, over IPv6 link-local on the management bridge (no address is
394: +#      added to the management network): sets the credentials and the identity,
395: +#      configures ether1 with the management addresses and keeps their return
396: +#      path in its own routing table (`mgmt`, below), then fetches
397: +#      /ftpboot/config.auto.rsc (the containerlab startup-config, when present)
398: +#      from the launcher and `/import`s it
399: +#   5. prints the ready marker on the container log and waits on qemu
400: +#
401: +# Restart (docker restart, containerlab restart --node): RouterOS keys an
402: +# ethernet interface on its MAC. A NIC that comes back with a new MAC is a NEW
403: +# device (ether1 becomes ether3) and the DHCP client bound to ether1 never
404: +# binds. Docker re-rolls the container's eth0 MAC whenever the netns is
405: +# recreated, so the node id that seeds every NIC MAC and the data NIC count are
406: +# persisted in the container on the first boot and reused: the restarted qemu
407: +# carries the same NICs in the same order and RouterOS keeps ether1..N and
408: +# their config. The REST bootstrap is skipped on a restart; the management
409: +# step re-runs and re-applies the container's current addressing.
410: +#
411: +# Architectures: the image's own qemu (qemu-system-x86_64 on amd64,
412: +#   qemu-system-aarch64 + edk2 on arm64), KVM when /dev/kvm exists, else TCG.
413: +# Args (the kind's Cmd): --hostname H [--username U --password P]
414: +#   (unknown flags are ignored); credentials default to the USERNAME/PASSWORD env
415: +# Env (all optional, set under the node's `env:`); MEM, CPUS and DISK are the
416: +#   official image's own variables:
417: +#   MEM (qemu -m value, default 256 MiB + 8 MiB per data NIC and vCPU), CPUS (default 1),
418: +#   DISK (default 128M), CHR_INTF_WAIT (s, 60), CHR_BOOT_WAIT (s, 300)
419: +# Serial console: port 5000 inside the container
420: +#   (docker exec -it <node> nc 127.0.0.1 5000); the container itself has no
421: +#   address on the management network.
422: +# Ready: /.chr-launch-ready exists once RouterOS is ready (the kind's default
423: +#   healthcheck); it is removed at every start.
424: +# Log lines (docker logs clab-<lab>-<node>), all prefixed "chr-launch:":
425: +#   "RouterOS ready"                   login + startup config done
426: +#   "startup config import FAILED: …"  the router rejected the .rsc (RouterOS's
427: +#                                      own reason, with the line number)
428: +#   "credential bootstrap FAILED: …"   the user/password step failed
429: +#   "management setup FAILED: …"       the ether1 address / mgmt table step failed
430: +#
431: +# Copyright 2026 NetPilot Labs
432: +# Licensed under the BSD 3-Clause License.
433: +# SPDX-License-Identifier: BSD-3-Clause
434: +
435: +set -u
436: +
437: +# Credentials come from the USERNAME/PASSWORD env the kind sets (no quoting
438: +# limits on the password); --username/--password still override them.
439: +USERNAME="${USERNAME:-admin}"
440: +PASSWORD="${PASSWORD-admin}"
441: +HOSTNAME_ARG=""
442: +while [ $# -gt 0 ]; do
443: +    case "$1" in
444: +        --username) USERNAME="$2"; shift 2 ;;
445: +        --password) PASSWORD="$2"; shift 2 ;;
446: +        --hostname) HOSTNAME_ARG="$2"; shift 2 ;;
447: +        --connection-mode) shift 2 ;;
448: +        --trace) shift ;;
449: +        *) shift ;;
450: +    esac
451: +done
452: +[ -n "$HOSTNAME_ARG" ] || HOSTNAME_ARG="$(hostname)"
453: +
454: +CPUS="${CPUS:-1}"
455: +DISK="${DISK:-128M}"
456: +INTF_WAIT="${CHR_INTF_WAIT:-60}"
457: +BOOT_WAIT="${CHR_BOOT_WAIT:-300}"
458: +INTFS="${CLAB_INTFS:-0}"
459: +STARTUP_CFG=/ftpboot/config.auto.rsc
460: +# Per-container state in the rw layer: survives every restart, gone with the
461: +# container (destroy, redeploy --cleanup).
462: +DONE_MARKER=/.chr-launch-bootstrapped
463: +READY_FILE=/.chr-launch-ready
464: +ID_FILE=/.chr-launch-node-id
465: +NICS_FILE=/.chr-launch-nics
466: +MGMT_TABLE=mgmt
467: +# every management object the launcher creates carries this comment, so each
468: +# boot can find and re-apply them, and SaveConfig can leave them out
469: +MGMT_COMMENT=clab-mgmt
470: +MGMT_BR=br0
471: +# eth0's addressing as last seen: a launcher re-run in the same network
472: +# namespace finds eth0 already bridged and bare
473: +MGMT_FILE=/.chr-launch-mgmt
474: +SYSNET=/sys/class/net
475: +
476: +log() { echo "chr-launch: $*"; }
477: +rm -f "$READY_FILE"
478: +
479: +FETCH_PORT=8080
480: +
481: +# ---- disk -----------------------------------------------------------------
482: +IMG=""
483: +for f in /diskimage/*.img; do [ -f "$f" ] && IMG="$f" && break; done
484: +if [ -z "$IMG" ]; then
485: +    log "no /diskimage/*.img in this image: not a RouterOS CHR image"
486: +    exit 1
487: +fi
488: +# Only GROW the disk to the floor: an image already larger keeps its tail.
489: +disk_bytes() {
490: +    case "$1" in
491: +        *G|*g) echo $(( ${1%[Gg]} * 1024 * 1024 * 1024 )) ;;
492: +        *M|*m) echo $(( ${1%[Mm]} * 1024 * 1024 )) ;;
493: +        *K|*k) echo $(( ${1%[Kk]} * 1024 )) ;;
494: +        *) echo "$1" ;;
495: +    esac
496: +}
497: +want_bytes="$(disk_bytes "$DISK")"
498: +cur_bytes="$(stat -c %s "$IMG" 2>/dev/null || wc -c < "$IMG")"
499: +if [ "$cur_bytes" -lt "$want_bytes" ] 2>/dev/null; then
500: +    truncate -s "$DISK" "$IMG"
501: +fi
502: +
503: +# ---- wait for containerlab's data interfaces ------------------------------
504: +# shellcheck disable=SC2012  # interface names are ethN, ls is safe here
505: +count_intfs() { ls -d "$SYSNET"/eth[1-9]* 2>/dev/null | wc -l; }
506: +waited=0
507: +while [ "$(count_intfs)" -lt "$INTFS" ] && [ "$waited" -lt "$INTF_WAIT" ]; do
508: +    sleep 1
509: +    waited=$((waited + 1))
510: +done
511: +log "interfaces: $(count_intfs) of $INTFS expected after ${waited}s"
512: +
513: +# ---- qemu command line ----------------------------------------------------
514: +# The image's own qemu and firmware, as its /start.sh selects them.
515: +case "$(uname -m)" in
516: +    aarch64|arm64)
517: +        QEMU="qemu-system-aarch64 -M virt -pflash /usr/share/qemu/edk2-aarch64-code.fd"
518: +        TCG_CPU="-cpu cortex-a57" ;;
519: +    *)
520: +        QEMU="qemu-system-x86_64"
521: +        TCG_CPU="" ;;
522: +esac
523: +if [ -e /dev/kvm ]; then
524: +    ACCEL="-cpu host -accel kvm"
525: +else
526: +    ACCEL="$TCG_CPU -accel tcg,split-wx=on"
527: +    log "no /dev/kvm: running under TCG emulation (slow boot)"
528: +fi
529: +
530: +# MACs: unique per node AND per NIC. The container's eth0 MAC is unique per
531: +# node, so its LAST TWO octets identify the node; the NIC's last byte is the
532: +# interface index (00 = mgmt, 01 = ether2, …). Two nodes sharing a NIC MAC on
533: +# a link drop each other's frames as their own. The id is read on the FIRST
534: +# boot only and persisted (see Restart in the header).
535: +node_id="$(cat "$ID_FILE" 2>/dev/null)"
536: +case "$node_id" in
537: +    [0-9a-f][0-9a-f]:[0-9a-f][0-9a-f]) ;;
538: +    *)
539: +        e="$(cat "$SYSNET/eth0/address" 2>/dev/null)"
540: +        if [ -n "$e" ]; then
541: +            o2="${e##*:}"; rest_mac="${e%:*}"; o1="${rest_mac##*:}"
542: +            node_id="$o1:$o2"
543: +        else
544: +            node_id="$(hexdump -n2 -e '2/1 "%02x:"' /dev/urandom | sed 's/:$//')"
545: +        fi
546: +        printf '%s\n' "$node_id" > "$ID_FILE" 2>/dev/null \
547: +            || log "node id $node_id not persisted: a restart will renumber the NICs"
548: +        ;;
549: +esac
550: +NET="-netdev tap,id=mgmt,ifname=tap0,script=no,downscript=no -device virtio-net,netdev=mgmt,mac=52:54:00:$node_id:00"
551: +
552: +# ---- management: eth0 + tap0 on one bridge, eth0's addressing to the VM ----
553: +ip_global() { # ip_global <4|6> <dev>: the first global address/prefix
554: +    ip -"$1" addr show dev "$2" 2>/dev/null \
555: +        | sed -n '/scope global/s/^ *inet6\{0,1\} \([^ ]*\) .*/\1/p' | head -n1
556: +}
557: +ip_gw() { # ip_gw <4|6>: the default gateway
558: +    ip -"$1" route show default 2>/dev/null | sed -n 's/^default via \([^ ]*\) .*/\1/p' | head -n1
559: +}
560: +M4="$(ip_global 4 eth0)"; M6="$(ip_global 6 eth0)"
561: +G4="$(ip_gw 4)"; G6="$(ip_gw 6)"
562: +if [ -n "$M4$M6" ]; then
563: +    printf 'M4=%s\nM6=%s\nG4=%s\nG6=%s\n' "$M4" "$M6" "$G4" "$G6" > "$MGMT_FILE" 2>/dev/null || true
564: +elif [ -f "$MGMT_FILE" ]; then
565: +    # shellcheck disable=SC1090  # written by this script, four plain assignments
566: +    . "$MGMT_FILE"
567: +fi
568: +log "management: ${M4:-no IPv4}${M6:+, $M6} via ${G4:-no gateway}${G6:+, $G6}"
569: +
570: +tunctl -t tap0 >/dev/null 2>&1 || ip tuntap add dev tap0 mode tap
571: +if [ ! -e "$SYSNET/$MGMT_BR" ]; then
572: +    _mtu="$(cat "$SYSNET/eth0/mtu" 2>/dev/null || echo 1500)"
573: +    ip addr flush dev eth0
574: +    # The launcher's side of the bootstrap channel: IPv6 link-local on the
575: +    # bridge only. eth0 and tap0 are plain bridge ports.
576: +    sysctl -qw net.ipv6.conf.default.disable_ipv6=0 net.ipv6.conf.default.accept_ra=0 2>/dev/null
577: +    brctl addbr "$MGMT_BR"
578: +    brctl setfd "$MGMT_BR" 0 2>/dev/null
579: +    echo 16384 > "$SYSNET/$MGMT_BR/bridge/group_fwd_mask" 2>/dev/null
580: +    ip link set "$MGMT_BR" address "52:54:01:$node_id:00"
581: +    ip link set tap0 mtu "$_mtu"
582: +    ip link set "$MGMT_BR" mtu "$_mtu"
583: +    # eth0 joins the bridge only once ether1 is configured (mgmt_attach): until
584: +    # then nothing the router sends (its boot-time router advertisements, CHR's
585: +    # DHCP discovers) reaches the management network.
586: +    brctl addif "$MGMT_BR" tap0
587: +    sysctl -qw net.ipv6.conf.eth0.disable_ipv6=1 net.ipv6.conf.tap0.disable_ipv6=1 \
588: +        "net.ipv6.conf.$MGMT_BR.disable_ipv6=0" "net.ipv6.conf.$MGMT_BR.accept_ra=0" \
589: +        "net.ipv6.conf.$MGMT_BR.router_solicitations=0" 2>/dev/null
590: +fi
591: +ip link set tap0 up
592: +ip link set "$MGMT_BR" up
593: +# RouterOS's ether1 link-local address, from the MAC this script gives it
594: +VM_LL="fe80::5054:ff:fe${node_id%%:*}:${node_id##*:}00"
595: +VM_EP="$VM_LL%$MGMT_BR"
596: +# The bridge's own link-local address (the startup-config fetch source). Read
597: +# once qemu runs: until then tap0, the bridge's only port, has no carrier and
598: +# the address stays tentative.
599: +LL=""
600: +bridge_ll() {
601: +    _w=0
602: +    while [ -z "$LL" ] && [ "$_w" -lt 20 ]; do
603: +        LL="$(ip -6 addr show dev "$MGMT_BR" 2>/dev/null | grep -v tentative \
604: +            | sed -n 's/^ *inet6 \(fe80::[^/]*\)\/64 .*/\1/p' | head -n1)"
605: +        [ -n "$LL" ] || { sleep 1; _w=$((_w + 1)); }
606: +    done
607: +    [ -n "$LL" ] || log "no IPv6 link-local on $MGMT_BR"
608: +}
609: +
610: +# NIC order = PCI order = the order of the -device args below. Walk
611: +# eth1..ethMAX so a gap (links on ether2 and ether5 only) keeps RouterOS
612: +# numbering aligned: a missing ethN still gets a tap, just no bridge. The count
613: +# never shrinks across restarts: a `docker restart` recreates the netns and
614: +# every lab veth of the node is gone, and a NIC absent for one boot loses the
615: +# config bound to it. The tap keeps ether(N+1) a real interface, its link comes
616: +# back when containerlab restores the veth or the lab is redeployed.
617: +max=0
618: +for d in "$SYSNET"/eth[1-9]*; do
619: +    [ -e "$d" ] || continue
620: +    n="${d##*/eth}"
621: +    [ "$n" -gt "$max" ] && max="$n"
622: +done
623: +prev="$(cat "$NICS_FILE" 2>/dev/null)"
624: +case "$prev" in *[!0-9]*|"") prev=0 ;; esac
625: +[ "$prev" -gt "$max" ] && max="$prev"
626: +# The wait above can end short of CLAB_INTFS on a large lab (links attach after
627: +# every container started): the NIC set still spans the expected count, and
628: +# the watcher bridges the late veths.
629: +case "$INTFS" in *[!0-9]*|"") INTFS=0 ;; esac
630: +[ "$INTFS" -gt "$max" ] && max="$INTFS"
631: +[ "$max" -gt "$prev" ] && { printf '%s\n' "$max" > "$NICS_FILE" 2>/dev/null || true; }
632: +
633: +# RouterOS v7 memory ≈ 256 + 8·CPUS·(ifaces) MiB (MikroTik CHR system
634: +# requirements). Scale by the HIGHEST generated NIC, not CLAB_INTFS: a sparse
635: +# link on ether64 means CLAB_INTFS=1 yet 63 data NICs are created below.
636: +if [ -z "${MEM:-}" ]; then
637: +    MEM=$((256 + 8 * CPUS * max))
638: +fi
639: +
640: +bridge_eth() { # bridge_eth <i>: container ethI joins brI beside tapI (brI made once)
641: +    _mtu="$(cat "$SYSNET/eth$1/mtu" 2>/dev/null || echo 1500)"
642: +    if [ ! -e "$SYSNET/br$1" ]; then
643: +        brctl addbr "br$1"
644: +        ip link set "br$1" mtu "$_mtu"
645: +        ip link set "tap$1" mtu "$_mtu"
646: +        # forward LLDP (01:80:c2:00:00:0e, bit 14); STP/LACP cannot cross a bridge
647: +        echo 16384 > "$SYSNET/br$1/bridge/group_fwd_mask" 2>/dev/null
648: +        brctl addif "br$1" "tap$1"
649: +    fi
650: +    brctl addif "br$1" "eth$1"
651: +    ip link set "eth$1" up
652: +    ip link set "br$1" up
653: +}
654: +# A veth that lands or returns while qemu runs (containerlab restores a link
655: +# right after a restart --node, a large lab attaches late, a peer node's
656: +# redeploy recreates the pair) joins its bridge as it appears, so the RouterOS
657: +# interface behind tapN gets its link back without a restart here.
658: +watch_links() {
659: +    _warned=""
660: +    while kill -0 "$QEMU_PID" 2>/dev/null; do
661: +        i=1
662: +        while [ "$i" -le "$max" ]; do
663: +            if [ -e "$SYSNET/eth$i" ] && [ ! -e "$SYSNET/br$i/brif/eth$i" ]; then
664: +                bridge_eth "$i" && log "link eth$i attached: bridged to tap$i"
665: +            fi
666: +            i=$((i + 1))
667: +        done
668: +        for d in "$SYSNET"/eth[1-9]*; do
669: +            [ -e "$d" ] || continue
670: +            n="${d##*/eth}"
671: +            [ "$n" -gt "$max" ] || continue
672: +            case " $_warned " in *" $n "*) continue ;; esac
673: +            _warned="$_warned $n"
674: +            log "link eth$n attached after qemu started with $max data NICs: no RouterOS interface for it until the node is restarted"
675: +        done
676: +        sleep 2
677: +    done
678: +}
679: +
680: +i=1
681: +while [ "$i" -le "$max" ]; do
682: +    tap="tap$i"
683: +    tunctl -t "$tap" >/dev/null 2>&1 || ip tuntap add dev "$tap" mode tap
684: +    [ -e "$SYSNET/eth$i" ] && bridge_eth "$i"
685: +    ip link set "$tap" up
686: +    mac="$(printf '52:54:00:%s:%02x' "$node_id" "$((i & 255))")"
687: +    NET="$NET -netdev tap,id=d$i,ifname=$tap,script=no,downscript=no -device virtio-net,netdev=d$i,mac=$mac"
688: +    i=$((i + 1))
689: +done
690: +
691: +# shellcheck disable=SC2086
692: +$QEMU $ACCEL -display none -monitor none \
693: +    -m "$MEM" -smp "cpus=$CPUS" \
694: +    -drive "file=$IMG,format=raw" \
695: +    -serial telnet:0.0.0.0:5000,server,nowait \
696: +    $NET &
697: +QEMU_PID=$!
698: +trap 'kill "$QEMU_PID" 2>/dev/null; wait "$QEMU_PID" 2>/dev/null; exit 0' TERM INT
699: +log "qemu started (pid $QEMU_PID, mem ${MEM}M, cpus $CPUS, data nics $max)"
700: +watch_links &
701: +
702: +# ---- REST helpers ---------------------------------------------------------
703: +json_str() { printf '%s' "$1" | sed 's/\\/\\\\/g; s/"/\\"/g'; }
704: +rest() { # rest <user:pass> <path> [json]
705: +    # Basic auth in a header, never in the URL: a password containing a URI
706: +    # delimiter (#, ?, /) would change where the authority ends. REST_T is the
707: +    # read timeout (default 20s).
708: +    _auth="Authorization: Basic $(printf '%s' "$1" | base64 | tr -d '\n')"
709: +    if [ $# -ge 3 ]; then
710: +        wget -q -O- -T "${REST_T:-20}" --header "Content-Type: application/json" \
711: +            --header "$_auth" --post-data "$3" "http://[$VM_EP]/rest/$2" 2>/dev/null
712: +    else
713: +        wget -q -O- -T "${REST_T:-20}" --header "$_auth" "http://[$VM_EP]/rest/$2" 2>/dev/null
714: +    fi
715: +}
716: +# busybox wget discards the body of an HTTP error response, and RouterOS puts
717: +# its diagnosis ONLY there ({"detail":"Script Error: … (line N column M)"}; its
718: +# /log stays silent). So the calls whose failure must be readable go over a raw
719: +# HTTP POST through busybox nc. busybox nc exits the moment stdin hits EOF,
720: +# reply or not, and RouterOS keeps the connection open despite
721: +# `Connection: close`, so the request side is held open on a fifo and closed
722: +# once Content-Length bytes of body have arrived.
723: +resp_complete() { # resp_complete <file>: headers + Content-Length bytes of body arrived
724: +    _cl="$(grep -i -m1 '^Content-Length:' "$1" 2>/dev/null | tr -dc '0-9')"
725: +    [ -n "$_cl" ] || return 1
726: +    grep -q -m1 '^\r*$' "$1" 2>/dev/null || return 1
727: +    [ "$(sed '1,/^\r*$/d' "$1" | wc -c | tr -d ' ')" -ge "$_cl" ]
728: +}
729: +rest_raw() { # rest_raw <user:pass> <path> <json> <timeout-s>  -> "<status> <body>"
730: +    _f="/tmp/chr-rest.$$"; rm -f "$_f" "$_f.out"; mkfifo "$_f" || return 1
731: +    timeout "$(( $4 + 5 ))" nc "$VM_EP" 80 <"$_f" >"$_f.out" 2>/dev/null &
732: +    _pid=$!
733: +    exec 7>"$_f"
734: +    printf 'POST /rest/%s HTTP/1.1\r\nHost: [%s]\r\nAuthorization: Basic %s\r\nContent-Type: application/json\r\nContent-Length: %s\r\nConnection: close\r\n\r\n%s' \
735: +        "$2" "$VM_LL" "$(printf '%s' "$1" | base64 | tr -d '\n')" \
736: +        "$(printf '%s' "$3" | wc -c | tr -d ' ')" "$3" >&7
737: +    _left=$4
738: +    while kill -0 "$_pid" 2>/dev/null && ! resp_complete "$_f.out" && [ "$_left" -gt 0 ]; do
739: +        sleep 1; _left=$(( _left - 1 ))
740: +    done
741: +    exec 7>&-
742: +    wait "$_pid" 2>/dev/null
743: +    _st="$(head -n1 "$_f.out" 2>/dev/null | tr -d '\r' | cut -d' ' -f2)"
744: +    _body="$(sed '1,/^\r*$/d' "$_f.out" 2>/dev/null | tr -d '\r')"
745: +    rm -f "$_f" "$_f.out"
746: +    printf '%s %s' "${_st:-000}" "$_body"
747: +}
748: +# RouterOS REST returns the object/array on success and an empty body for a
749: +# bare `set`: only an explicit error object is a failure here. A silently
750: +# failed call is caught by the read-back checks that follow each step.
751: +is_err() { case "$1" in *'"error"'*) return 0 ;; *) return 1 ;; esac; }
752: +
753: +# ---- wait for RouterOS ----------------------------------------------------
754: +waited=0
755: +AUTH=""
756: +# A fresh official CHR disk has ONLY the factory `admin` account (blank
757: +# password): the first boot authenticates as that account whatever --username
758: +# asks for, and the requested user is created below. On a restart the pair that
759: +# answers is the requested user.
760: +while [ -z "$AUTH" ]; do
761: +    for _cred in "admin:" "admin:$PASSWORD" "$USERNAME:$PASSWORD"; do
762: +        rest "$_cred" system/resource >/dev/null 2>&1 && { AUTH="$_cred"; break; }
763: +    done
764: +    [ -n "$AUTH" ] && break
765: +    if ! kill -0 "$QEMU_PID" 2>/dev/null; then
766: +        log "qemu exited during boot"
767: +        exit 1
768: +    fi
769: +    sleep 2
770: +    waited=$((waited + 2))
771: +    if [ "$waited" -ge "$BOOT_WAIT" ]; then
772: +        log "RouterOS did not answer within ${BOOT_WAIT}s: leaving qemu running"
773: +        wait "$QEMU_PID"
774: +        exit 1
775: +    fi
776: +done
777: +log "RouterOS answered after ${waited}s"
778: +
779: +# Ready means a user can log in: RouterOS starts its SSH server well after REST
780: +# answers on a slow boot (about 40 s later under TCG on arm64), so when the
781: +# configuration keeps SSH enabled, its port must send the banner first.
782: +# A config that disables SSH is not waited for; a banner that never comes is
783: +# logged and the node is still declared ready (REST works).
784: +ssh_banner() { (sleep 3) | nc "$VM_EP" 22 2>/dev/null | head -c 7; }
785: +ready() {
786: +    case "$(rest "$AUTH" "ip/service?name=ssh")" in
787: +        *'"disabled":"false"'*)
788: +            _t0=$(date +%s)
789: +            until [ "$(ssh_banner)" = "SSH-2.0" ]; do
790: +                if [ $(( $(date +%s) - _t0 )) -ge "$BOOT_WAIT" ]; then
791: +                    log "SSH did not answer within $(( $(date +%s) - _t0 ))s (REST works)"
792: +                    break
793: +                fi
794: +                sleep 2
795: +            done
796: +            ;;
797: +    esac
798: +    touch "$READY_FILE" 2>/dev/null || true
799: +    log "RouterOS ready"
800: +}
801: +
802: +# A failed step WITHHOLDS the ready marker (qemu keeps running, so the node can
803: +# still be inspected over the serial console, port 5000 in the container).
804: +bootstrap_fail() {
805: +    log "credential bootstrap FAILED: $1"
806: +    wait "$QEMU_PID"
807: +    exit 0
808: +}
809: +
810: +# ---- management: ether1 addressing and its own routing table ----------------
811: +# ether1 gets the container's management addresses, and their return path lives
812: +# in a `mgmt` routing table selected by a rule on those source addresses: a
813: +# default route the lab adds to `main` (a static 0.0.0.0/0, a PPPoE client's
814: +# default route, a dual-WAN pair) would otherwise take SSH/REST replies away
815: +# from the management network while the data plane works. CHR's default ether1
816: +# DHCP client is disabled: the address is static. Everything is tagged
817: +# clab-mgmt and re-applied on every boot from the container's CURRENT addressing
818: +# (a restart can bring a new IP), while the bootstrap channel itself
819: +# (link-local) never depends on it.
820: +mgmt_fail() {
821: +    log "management setup FAILED: $1 (redeploy the node: containerlab deploy --reconfigure)"
822: +    wait "$QEMU_PID"
823: +    exit 0
824: +}
825: +rest_has() { # rest_has <path?query>: the GET answered a non-empty JSON array
826: +    case "$(rest "$AUTH" "$1")" in '['*'{'*) return 0 ;; *) return 1 ;; esac
827: +}
828: +rest_add() { # rest_add <what> <path> <json>: a 2xx with no error object, else mgmt_fail with the router's detail
829: +    _r="$(rest_raw "$AUTH" "$2" "$3" 20)"
830: +    case "$_r" in
831: +        2??\ *) is_err "$_r" && mgmt_fail "$1: ${_r#* }" ;;
832: +        *) mgmt_fail "$1: ${_r:-no response}" ;;
833: +    esac
834: +}
835: +rest_count() { # rest_count <path?query>: number of objects the GET answered
836: +    rest "$AUTH" "$1" | tr '{' '\n' | grep -c '"\.id"'
837: +}
838: +rest_clear() { # rest_clear <menu>: remove its clab-mgmt objects
839: +    _ids="$(rest "$AUTH" "$1?comment=$MGMT_COMMENT" | tr '{' '\n' \
840: +        | sed -n 's/.*"\.id":"\([^"]*\)".*/\1/p' | paste -sd, -)"
841: +    [ -z "$_ids" ] || rest_add "remove old $1" "$1/remove" "{\".id\":\"$_ids\"}"
842: +}
843: +# The tagged objects already match the container's addressing (a restart with
844: +# the same IP): leave them alone, re-applying would only drop live sessions.
845: +mgmt_in_place() {
846: +    _want=0
847: +    if [ -n "$M4" ]; then
848: +        rest_has "ip/address?interface=ether1&address=$M4&comment=$MGMT_COMMENT" || return 1
849: +        rest_has "routing/rule?src-address=${M4%/*}/32&table=$MGMT_TABLE" || return 1
850: +        [ -z "$G4" ] || rest_has "ip/route?routing-table=$MGMT_TABLE&dst-address=0.0.0.0/0&gateway=$G4" || return 1
851: +        _want=$((_want + 1))
852: +    fi
853: +    if [ -n "$M6" ]; then
854: +        rest_has "ipv6/address?interface=ether1&address=$M6&comment=$MGMT_COMMENT" || return 1
855: +        _want=$((_want + 1))
856: +    fi
857: +    [ "$(rest_count "ip/address?comment=$MGMT_COMMENT")$(rest_count "ipv6/address?comment=$MGMT_COMMENT")" \
858: +        = "$([ -n "$M4" ] && echo 1 || echo 0)$([ -n "$M6" ] && echo 1 || echo 0)" ] || return 1
859: +    rest_has "ipv6/nd?interface=ether1&ra-lifetime=none" || return 1
860: +    [ "$_want" -gt 0 ]
861: +}
862: +mgmt_setup() {
863: +    if mgmt_in_place; then
864: +        log "management on ether1 unchanged: ${M4:-no IPv4}${M6:+, $M6}"
865: +        return 0
866: +    fi
867: +    for _m in routing/rule ip/route ipv6/route ip/address ipv6/address; do rest_clear "$_m"; done
868: +    rest_has "routing/table?name=$MGMT_TABLE" \
869: +        || rest_add "routing table $MGMT_TABLE" routing/table/add \
870: +            "{\"name\":\"$MGMT_TABLE\",\"fib\":\"yes\",\"comment\":\"$MGMT_COMMENT\"}"
871: +    _c="\"comment\":\"$MGMT_COMMENT\""
872: +    if [ -n "$M4" ]; then
873: +        _net4="$(ipcalc -n "$M4" | sed -n 's/^NETWORK=//p')/${M4#*/}"
874: +        rest_add "ether1 address $M4" ip/address/add "{\"address\":\"$M4\",\"interface\":\"ether1\",$_c}"
875: +        rest_add "route $_net4 in table $MGMT_TABLE" ip/route/add \
876: +            "{\"dst-address\":\"$_net4\",\"gateway\":\"ether1\",\"routing-table\":\"$MGMT_TABLE\",$_c}"
877: +        [ -z "$G4" ] || rest_add "default via $G4 in table $MGMT_TABLE" ip/route/add \
878: +            "{\"dst-address\":\"0.0.0.0/0\",\"gateway\":\"$G4\",\"routing-table\":\"$MGMT_TABLE\",$_c}"
879: +        rest_add "routing rule for ${M4%/*}" routing/rule/add \
880: +            "{\"src-address\":\"${M4%/*}/32\",\"action\":\"lookup-only-in-table\",\"table\":\"$MGMT_TABLE\",$_c}"
881: +    fi
882: +    if [ -n "$M6" ]; then
883: +        rest_add "ether1 address $M6" ipv6/address/add \
884: +            "{\"address\":\"$M6\",\"interface\":\"ether1\",\"advertise\":\"no\",$_c}"
885: +        # RouterOS normalizes the host bits of dst-address (::3/64 -> ::/64)
886: +        rest_add "IPv6 route $M6 in table $MGMT_TABLE" ipv6/route/add \
887: +            "{\"dst-address\":\"$M6\",\"gateway\":\"ether1\",\"routing-table\":\"$MGMT_TABLE\",$_c}"
888: +        [ -z "$G6" ] || rest_add "IPv6 default via $G6 in table $MGMT_TABLE" ipv6/route/add \
889: +            "{\"dst-address\":\"::/0\",\"gateway\":\"$G6%ether1\",\"routing-table\":\"$MGMT_TABLE\",$_c}"
890: +        rest_add "routing rule for ${M6%/*}" routing/rule/add \
891: +            "{\"src-address\":\"${M6%/*}/128\",\"action\":\"lookup-only-in-table\",\"table\":\"$MGMT_TABLE\",$_c}"
892: +    fi
893: +    _id="$(rest "$AUTH" "ip/dhcp-client?interface=ether1" | sed -n 's/.*"\.id":"\([^"]*\)".*/\1/p' | head -n1)"
894: +    [ -z "$_id" ] || rest_add "disable the ether1 DHCP client" ip/dhcp-client/set \
895: +        "{\"numbers\":\"$_id\",\"disabled\":\"yes\"}"
896: +    # RouterOS's default ND entry (interface=all, ra-lifetime=30m) sends router
897: +    # advertisements on ether1 too, and every host on the management network
898: +    # with accept_ra would install the router as its IPv6 default route. An
899: +    # ether1 entry with ra-lifetime=none keeps the RAs harmless and withdraws a
900: +    # route a host already learned (a disabled entry does not override "all").
901: +    # /ipv6/nd keeps no comment, so its entry is found by interface.
902: +    _nd="$(rest "$AUTH" "ipv6/nd?interface=ether1" | sed -n 's/.*"\.id":"\([^"]*\)".*/\1/p' | head -n1)"
903: +    if [ -n "$_nd" ]; then
904: +        rest_add "ether1 router advertisements off" ipv6/nd/set \
905: +            "{\"numbers\":\"$_nd\",\"ra-lifetime\":\"none\",\"disabled\":\"no\"}"
906: +    else
907: +        rest_add "ether1 router advertisements off" ipv6/nd/add \
908: +            "{\"interface\":\"ether1\",\"ra-lifetime\":\"none\"}"
909: +    fi
910: +    # Prove they took (a transport flake answers an empty body, which is not
911: +    # an error by the set-contract).
912: +    if [ -n "$M4" ]; then
913: +        rest_has "ip/address?interface=ether1&address=$M4" || mgmt_fail "$M4 not on ether1"
914: +        rest_has "routing/rule?src-address=${M4%/*}/32&table=$MGMT_TABLE" \
915: +            || mgmt_fail "routing rule for ${M4%/*} not applied"
916: +    fi
917: +    log "management on ether1: ${M4:-no IPv4}${M6:+, $M6}, return path in routing table $MGMT_TABLE"
918: +}
919: +# Join the management network, then have the router reach its gateway from the
920: +# management address: proves the return path both ways, and the ARP/ND request
921: +# refreshes the gateway's neighbor entry when the IP was used by another MAC
922: +# before (a redeployed lab hands out the same addresses).
923: +mgmt_attach() {
924: +    [ -e "$SYSNET/$MGMT_BR/brif/eth0" ] || brctl addif "$MGMT_BR" eth0
925: +    ip link set eth0 up
926: +    for _pair in "$G4 ${M4%/*}" "$G6 ${M6%/*}"; do
927: +        # shellcheck disable=SC2086  # split "gateway source"; an empty half skips the pair
928: +        set -- $_pair
929: +        [ $# -eq 2 ] || continue
930: +        _t0=$(date +%s)
931: +        until case "$(rest "$AUTH" ping "{\"address\":\"$1\",\"src-address\":\"$2\",\"count\":\"2\"}")" in
932: +                *'"received":"'[1-9]*) true ;; *) false ;; esac; do
933: +            if [ $(( $(date +%s) - _t0 )) -ge 30 ]; then
934: +                log "gateway $1 does not answer from $2 after $(( $(date +%s) - _t0 ))s (continuing)"
935: +                break
936: +            fi
937: +            sleep 1
938: +        done
939: +    done
940: +}
941: +
942: +# Bootstrap + startup-config import happen ONCE per container lifetime. On a
943: +# restart the CHR disk keeps its config and the marker (in the container rw
944: +# layer) survives, so the bootstrap (which could wait forever if the config
945: +# disabled `www` or changed the account, or reject duplicate `add` lines) is
946: +# skipped. A fresh deploy recreates the container and the disk, so the marker
947: +# and the config are both gone and the bootstrap runs.
948: +if [ -f "$DONE_MARKER" ]; then
949: +    log "already bootstrapped (restart): skipping config"
950: +    mgmt_setup
951: +    mgmt_attach
952: +    ready
953: +    wait "$QEMU_PID"
954: +    exit 0
955: +fi
956: +
957: +# ---- credentials + identity -----------------------------------------------
958: +# Every credential-establishing call is CHECKED: the ready marker never
959: +# advertises credentials that do not work.
960: +FACTORY="admin:"
961: +rest "$FACTORY" system/resource >/dev/null 2>&1 || FACTORY="admin:$PASSWORD"
962: +if [ "$USERNAME" = "admin" ]; then
963: +    if [ -n "$PASSWORD" ]; then
964: +        out="$(rest "$FACTORY" user/set \
965: +            "{\"numbers\":\"admin\",\"password\":\"$(json_str "$PASSWORD")\"}")"
966: +        is_err "$out" && bootstrap_fail "set admin password: ${out:-no response}"
967: +    fi
968: +    AUTH="admin:$PASSWORD"
969: +else
970: +    out="$(rest "$FACTORY" user/add \
971: +        "{\"name\":\"$(json_str "$USERNAME")\",\"password\":\"$(json_str "$PASSWORD")\",\"group\":\"full\"}")"
972: +    is_err "$out" && bootstrap_fail "create user $USERNAME: ${out:-no response}"
973: +    AUTH="$USERNAME:$PASSWORD"
974: +    # The factory `admin` still has a BLANK password and is reachable over the
975: +    # forwarded services: disable it so the node keeps no unauthenticated
976: +    # full-access account.
977: +    out="$(rest "$AUTH" user/set "{\"numbers\":\"admin\",\"disabled\":\"yes\"}")"
978: +    is_err "$out" && bootstrap_fail "disable factory admin: ${out:-no response}"
979: +    if rest "admin:" system/resource >/dev/null 2>&1; then
980: +        bootstrap_fail "factory admin still authenticates after disable"
981: +    fi
982: +fi
983: +rest "$AUTH" system/resource >/dev/null 2>&1 \
984: +    || bootstrap_fail "the requested credentials do not authenticate"
985: +out="$(rest "$AUTH" system/identity/set "{\"name\":\"$(json_str "$HOSTNAME_ARG")\"}")"
986: +is_err "$out" && bootstrap_fail "set identity: ${out:-no response}"
987: +got="$(rest "$AUTH" system/identity)"
988: +case "$got" in
989: +    *"\"name\":\"$HOSTNAME_ARG\""*) : ;;
990: +    *) bootstrap_fail "identity not applied: ${got:-no response}" ;;
991: +esac
992: +
993: +mgmt_setup
994: +mgmt_attach
995: +
996: +# ---- startup config -------------------------------------------------------
997: +if [ -s "$STARTUP_CFG" ]; then
998: +    bridge_ll
999: +    # one-shot HTTP server on the bridge's link-local address; the router
1000: +    # fetches from it through ether1 (fetch takes a zoned link-local address
1001: +    # only as `address=`, not inside a URL)
1002: +    {
1003: +        printf 'HTTP/1.0 200 OK\r\nContent-Type: text/plain\r\nContent-Length: %s\r\n\r\n' \
1004: +            "$(wc -c < "$STARTUP_CFG")"
1005: +        cat "$STARTUP_CFG"
1006: +    } | timeout 90 nc -l -s "$LL%$MGMT_BR" -p "$FETCH_PORT" >/dev/null 2>&1 &
1007: +    sleep 1
1008: +    fetch="$(rest "$AUTH" tool/fetch \
1009: +        "{\"address\":\"$LL%ether1\",\"port\":\"$FETCH_PORT\",\"src-path\":\"/config.auto.rsc\",\"mode\":\"http\",\"dst-path\":\"config.auto.rsc\"}")"
1010: +    case "$fetch" in
1011: +        *'"status":"finished"'*)
1012: +            # /import answers only when it finishes: a large config gets
1013: +            # CHR_BOOT_WAIT seconds.
1014: +            result="$(rest_raw "$AUTH" import '{"file-name":"config.auto.rsc"}' "$BOOT_WAIT")"
1015: +            case "$result" in
1016: +                *'"ret":"true"'*)
1017: +                    log "startup config imported"
1018: +                    ;;
1019: +                *)
1020: +                    # RouterOS imports line by line and stops at the first
1021: +                    # error: the lines before it ARE applied.
1022: +                    detail="$(printf '%s' "$result" | sed -n 's/.*"detail":"\([^"]*\)".*/\1/p')"
1023: +                    log "startup config import FAILED: ${detail:-${result:-no response}}; lines before the failing one were applied, the rest were not"
1024: +                    wait "$QEMU_PID"
1025: +                    exit 0
1026: +                    ;;
1027: +            esac
1028: +            ;;
1029: +        *)
1030: +            log "startup config import FAILED: fetch did not finish: ${fetch:-no response}"
1031: +            wait "$QEMU_PID"
1032: +            exit 0
1033: +            ;;
1034: +    esac
1035: +fi
