+kind_display_name: MikroTik RouterOS CHR\n197: +---\n198: +# -{{ kind_display_name }}-\n199: +\n200: +[MikroTik RouterOS](https://mikrotik.com/software) Cloud Hosted Router (CHR) from the **official [`mikrotik/chr`](https://hub.docker.com/r/mikrotik/chr) image** is identified with the `-{{ kind_code_name }}-` kind in the [topology file](../topo-def-file.md). The image is published by MikroTik for `amd64` and `arm64` and is used as is: no [vrnetlab](../vrnetlab.md) build is needed.\n201: +\n202: +The image is a QEMU VM (Alpine, qemu and the CHR disk) in a container. Its own `/start.sh` is not made for orchestrated links ([#3116](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/issues/3116)), so the kind writes a launcher script into the node's lab directory, mounts it read-only and runs it as the entrypoint instead. The launcher uses only the tools that ship in the image.\n203: +\n204: +/// note\n205: +The [`mikrotik_ros`](vr-ros.md) kind runs CHR from an image you build with vrnetlab. Both kinds use the same interface naming and startup config format, so a lab moves from one to the other by changing `kind` and `image`.\n206: +///\n207: +\n208: +## Getting -{{ kind_display_name }}- image\n209: +\n210: +```bash\n211: +docker pull mikrotik/chr:7.24.5\n212: +```\n213: +\n214: +Tags follow RouterOS versions, plus `stable`, `long-term`, `testing` and `development`. This kind has been tested with 7.24.5 (stable), 7.23.8 (long-term) and 7.25rc2 (testing).\n215: +\n216: +## Managing -{{ kind_display_name }}- nodes\n217: +\n218: +/// tab | CLI\n219: +```bash\n220: +ssh admin@<node-name>\n221: +```\n222: +///\n223: +/// tab | REST API\n224: +```bash\n225: +curl -u admin:admin http://<node-name>/rest/system/resource\n226: +```\n227: +///\n228: +/// tab | Winbox / API\n229: +Winbox (TCP 8291) and the RouterOS API (TCP 8728, 8729 for TLS) are reachable on the node's management address.\n230: +///\n231: +/// tab | Console\n232: +The serial console is on port 5000 inside the container:\n233: +```bash\n234: +docker exec -it <node-name> nc 127.0.0.1 5000\n235: +```\n236: +///\n237: +/// tab | bash\n238: +```bash\n239: +docker exec -it <node-name> sh\n240: +```\n241: +///\n242: +\n243: +The node's management address is RouterOS's own `ether1` address ([transparent management](#management-connectivity)), so every service RouterOS runs answers on it directly, with nothing forwarded.\n244: +\n245: +/// note\n246: +Default credentials are `admin:admin`. With custom [`credentials`](../nodes.md#credentials) the kind creates that user in the `full` group and disables the factory `admin` account, which has a blank password on a fresh CHR disk.\n247: +///\n248: +\n249: +## Interface naming\n250: +\n251: +You can use [interface names](../topo-def-file.md#interface-naming) in the topology file like they appear in RouterOS: `etherX`, where `X` is the port number.\n252: +\n253: +* `ether2` - first data port\n254: +* `ether3` - second data port, and so on\n255: +\n256: +/// admonition\n257: +    type: warning\n258: +Data ports start at `ether2`: `ether1` is the management interface. Using `ether1` in a topology results in an error.\n259: +///\n260: +\n261: +The container interface `ethN` is RouterOS `ether(N+1)`, also when links leave gaps: a link on `eth12` alone still lands on `ether13`, with `ether2` to `ether12` present and unlinked. Interface MTU follows the link MTU, and LLDP frames are forwarded, so `/ip neighbor` shows the peers.\n262: +\n263: +## Node configuration\n264: +\n265: +-{{ kind_display_name }}- nodes boot with the default CHR configuration plus the credentials, the identity (set to the node name) and the management setup described below.\n266: +\n267: +### Startup configuration\n268: +\n269: +A RouterOS script (`.rsc`) set with [`startup-config`](../nodes.md#startup-config) is imported once the router is up:\n270: +\n271: +```yaml\n272: +name: chr_lab\n273: +topology:\n274: +  nodes:\n275: +    r1:\n276: +      kind: mikrotik_chr\n277: +      image: mikrotik/chr:7.24.5\n278: +      startup-config: r1.rsc\n279: +```\n280: +\n281: +The file is copied to the node's lab directory as `ftpboot/config.auto.rsc` and imported over the REST API. RouterOS imports line by line and stops at the first error: in that case the container log carries RouterOS's own reason with the line number, for example\n282: +\n283: +```\n284: +chr-launch: startup config import FAILED: Script Error: bad parameter network-type (line 3 column 59); lines before the failing one were applied, the rest were not\n285: +```\n286: +\n287: +and the node does not become ready.\n288: +\n289: +### Saving configuration\n290: +\n291: +[`containerlab save`](../../cmd/save.md) writes each node's `/export` to `ftpboot/config.auto.rsc` in its lab directory. The management objects the launcher creates at every boot (everything tagged `clab-mgmt`, and the `ether1` DHCP client) are left out, so the saved file can be used as the startup config of the next deployment, also one with different management addresses.\n292: +\n293: +### Node readiness\n294: +\n295: +The node is ready once RouterOS accepted the credentials and the startup config, reaches its gateway from the management address and, unless the configuration disables SSH, its SSH server answers. An SSH server that does not answer within `CHR_BOOT_WAIT` is logged, and the node is still declared ready, since REST and the console work. The container log then shows\n296: +\n297: +```\n298: +chr-launch: RouterOS ready\n299: +```\n300: +\n301: +and the kind's default [healthcheck](../nodes.md#healthcheck) reports the container `healthy`, so [`stages`](../nodes.md#stages) with `wait-for` can depend on it. A node whose startup config was rejected never becomes healthy. A `healthcheck` set in the topology replaces the default one.\n302: +\n303: +### Resources\n304: +\n305: +The kind reads the official image's own environment variables:\n306: +\n307: +| Variable | Default | Purpose |\n308: +|----------|---------|---------|\n309: +| `MEM` | 256 MiB + 8 MiB per data interface and vCPU | VM memory (a QEMU `-m` value, e.g. `1024` or `1G`) |\n310: +| `CPUS` | `1` | VM vCPUs |\n311: +| `DISK` | `128M` | minimum disk size (the disk only grows) |\n312: +| `CHR_INTF_WAIT` | `60` | seconds to wait for the data interfaces containerlab attaches before the VM starts |\n313: +| `CHR_BOOT_WAIT` | `300` | seconds to wait for RouterOS to answer, for the startup config import, and for SSH before the node is declared ready |\n314: +\n315: +```yaml\n316: +r1:\n317: +  kind: mikrotik_chr\n318: +  image: mikrotik/chr:7.24.5\n319: +  env:\n320: +    MEM: 1024\n321: +    CPUS: 2\n322: +```\n323: +\n324: +KVM is used when `/dev/kvm` is available. Without it the image's QEMU runs under TCG emulation: the node works, but boots several times slower.\n325: +\n326: +## Management connectivity\n327: +\n328: +Management is transparent: the IPv4 and IPv6 addresses and gateways containerlab gives the node's `eth0` are configured on RouterOS `ether1`, and the container's `eth0` is bridged to the VM's `ether1`. The router is the node's management address: SSH, the REST API, Winbox, SNMP and every other service answer on it, LLDP/MNDP work on the management network, and the address in the topology table is the one in the RouterOS configuration.\n329: +\n330: +The image ships busybox only (no `tc`), so where vrnetlab's [transparent management](../vrnetlab.md#management-interface) redirects with tc-mirred, this kind uses a Linux bridge. The container itself has no address on the management network; it reaches RouterOS for bootstrap over IPv6 link-local on that bridge.\n331: +\n332: +The management return path lives in its own routing table, `mgmt`, selected by routing rules for the management addresses, and CHR's `ether1` DHCP client is disabled. A default route in the lab configuration, a PPPoE client's default route or a dual-WAN setup in `main` therefore does not cut SSH or REST access to the node. RouterOS's router advertisements on `ether1` carry a zero router lifetime, so no host on the management network takes the router as its IPv6 default gateway; the container joins the management network only once this is in place. The objects carry the comment `clab-mgmt` and are re-applied at boot when the container's addressing changed.\n333: +\n334: +/// warning\n335: +Do not remove or change the `clab-mgmt` objects or the `mgmt` routing table: management of the node depends on them.\n336: +///\n337: +\n338: +## Restarts\n339: +\n340: +[`containerlab restart`](../../cmd/restart.md) and a `/system reboot` inside RouterOS keep the interface names, MAC addresses, configuration and links: the launcher persists the NIC identity in the container on first boot and does not re-run the bootstrap. A plain `docker restart` keeps the router and its management, but Docker deletes the node's veth links, which stay down until the node is restarted with containerlab or the lab is redeployed.\n341: +\n342: +## File mounts\n343: +\n344: +For each node containerlab creates in its [lab directory](../conf-artifacts.md):\n345: +\n346: +* `ftpboot/` - mounted to `/ftpboot`, holds `config.auto.rsc`\n347: +* `chr-launch.sh` - the launcher, mounted read-only to `/chr-launch.sh` and rewritten on every deploy\n348: diff --git a/mkdocs.yml b/mkdocs.yml\n349: index 914ad86..d6273e0 100644\n350: --- a/mkdocs.yml\n351: +++ b/mkdocs.yml\n352: @@ -46,10 +46,11 @@ nav:\n353:            - FTOS10v: manual/kinds/vr-ftosv.md\n354:            - Enterprise SONiC: manual/kinds/dell_sonic.md\n355:        - PLVision:\n356:            - Enterprise SONiC: manual/kinds/plvision_sonic.md\n357:        - MikroTik RouterOS: manual/kinds/vr-ros.md\n358: +      - MikroTik RouterOS CHR (official image): manual/kinds/mikrotik_chr.md\n359:        - Huawei VRP: manual/kinds/huawei_vrp.md\n360:        - IPInfusion OcNOS: manual/kinds/ipinfusion-ocnos.md\n361:        - VyOS Networks VyOS: manual/kinds/vyosnetworks_vyos.md\n362:        - OpenBSD: manual/kinds/openbsd.md\n363:        - FreeBSD: manual/kinds/freebsd.md\n364: diff --git a/nodes/mikrotik_chr/chr-launch.sh b/nodes/mikrotik_chr/chr-launch.sh\n365: new file mode 100644\n366: index 0000000..aa039f4\n367: --- /dev/null\n368: +++ b/nodes/mikrotik_chr/chr-launch.sh\n369: @@ -0,0 +1,670 @@\n370: +#!/bin/sh\n371: +# chr-launch.sh: run the OFFICIAL mikrotik/chr image (MikroTik RouterOS Cloud\n372: +# Hosted Router) as a containerlab node, image untouched.\n373: +#\n374: +# The image is MikroTik's own Docker Hub distribution (Alpine + qemu +\n375: +# /diskimage/chr-<ver>.img + /start.sh). This script is bind-mounted into the\n376: +# node and set as its entrypoint by the containerlab `mikrotik_chr` kind. It replaces\n377: +# the image's /start.sh, which is not orchestration-ready (containerlab#3116):\n378: +# it scans interfaces once before containerlab attaches the links, bridges the\n379: +# management NIC so the container IP never reaches the router, and imports no\n380: +# startup config.\n381: +#\n382: +# What it does (vrnetlab's mikrotik/routeros launch.py is the model, re-done\n383: +# with the image's own busybox + qemu: no python, no ftp client, no tc):\n384: +#   1. waits for the CLAB_INTFS data interfaces containerlab attaches (eth1..N)\n385: +#   2. management, transparent: the container's eth0 is bridged with the VM's\n386: +#      ether1 tap, and eth0's addresses and default gateways move into RouterOS\n387: +#      ether1. The router IS the node's management address: every service and\n388: +#      LLDP reach it directly, nothing is port-forwarded. The image ships only\n389: +#      busybox (no tc), so this uses a bridge where vrnetlab uses tc-mirred\n390: +#   3. data: one tap per container interface, bridged pairwise (ethN <-> tapN,\n391: +#      LLDP forwarded). NIC order in the VM follows the eth index, so container\n392: +#      ethN is always RouterOS ether(N+1), even with gaps\n393: +#   4. after boot, over IPv6 link-local on the management bridge (no address is\n394: +#      added to the management network): sets the credentials and the identity,\n395: +#      configures ether1 with the management addresses and keeps their return\n396: +#      path in its own routing table (`mgmt`, below), then fetches\n397: +#      /ftpboot/config.auto.rsc (the containerlab startup-config, when present)\n398: +#      from the launcher and `/import`s it\n399: +#   5. prints the ready marker on the container log and waits on qemu\n400: +#\n401: +# Restart (docker restart, containerlab restart --node): RouterOS keys an\n402: +# ethernet interface on its MAC. A NIC that comes back with a new MAC is a NEW\n403: +# device (ether1 becomes ether3) and the DHCP client bound to ether1 never\n404: +# binds. Docker re-rolls the container's eth0 MAC whenever the netns is\n405: +# recreated, so the node id that seeds every NIC MAC and the data NIC count are\n406: +# persisted in the container on the first boot and reused: the restarted qemu\n407: +# carries the same NICs in the same order and RouterOS keeps ether1..N and\n408: +# their config. The REST bootstrap is skipped on a restart; the management\n409: +# step re-runs and re-applies the container's current addressing.\n410: +#\n411: +# Architectures: the image's own qemu (qemu-system-x86_64 on amd64,\n412: +#   qemu-system-aarch64 + edk2 on arm64), KVM when /dev/kvm exists, else TCG.\n413: +# Args (the kind's Cmd): --hostname H [--username U --password P]\n414: +#   (unknown flags are ignored); credentials default to the USERNAME/PASSWORD env\n415: +# Env (all optional, set under the node's `env:`); MEM, CPUS and DISK are the\n416: +#   official image's own variables:\n417: +#   MEM (qemu -m value, default 256 MiB + 8 MiB per data NIC and vCPU), CPUS (default 1),\n418: +#   DISK (default 128M), CHR_INTF_WAIT (s, 60), CHR_BOOT_WAIT (s, 300)\n419: +# Serial console: port 5000 inside the container\n420: +#   (docker exec -it <node> nc 127.0.0.1 5000); the container itself has no\n421: +#   address on the management network.\n422: +# Ready: /.chr-launch-ready exists once RouterOS is ready (the kind's default\n423: +#   healthcheck); it is removed at every start.\n424: +# Log lines (docker logs clab-<lab>-<node>), all prefixed \"chr-launch:\":\n425: +#   \"RouterOS ready\"                   login + startup config done\n426: +#   \"startup config import FAILED: …\"  the router rejected the .rsc (RouterOS's\n427: +#                                      own reason, with the line number)\n428: +#   \"credential bootstrap FAILED: …\"   the user/password step failed\n429: +#   \"management setup FAILED: …\"       the ether1 address / mgmt table step failed\n430: +#\n431: +# Copyright 2026 NetPilot Labs\n432: +# Licensed under the BSD 3-Clause License.\n433: +# SPDX-License-Identifier: BSD-3-Clause\n434: +\n435: +set -u\n436: +\n437: +# Credentials come from the USERNAME/PASSWORD env the kind sets (no quoting\n438: +# limits on the password); --username/--password still override them.\n439: +USERNAME=\"${USERNAME:-admin}\"\n440: +PASSWORD=\"${PASSWORD-admin}\"\n441: +HOSTNAME_ARG=\"\"\n442: +while [ $# -gt 0 ]; do\n443: +    case \"$1\" in\n444: +        --username) USERNAME=\"$2\"; shift 2 ;;\n445: +        --password) PASSWORD=\"$2\"; shift 2 ;;\n446: +        --hostname) HOSTNAME_ARG=\"$2\"; shift 2 ;;\n447: +        --connection-mode) shift 2 ;;\n448: +        --trace) shift ;;\n449: +        *) shift ;;\n450: +    esac\n451: +done\n452: +[ -n \"$HOSTNAME_ARG\" ] || HOSTNAME_ARG=\"$(hostname)\"\n453: +\n454: +CPUS=\"${CPUS:-1}\"\n455: +DISK=\"${DISK:-128M}\"\n456: +INTF_WAIT=\"${CHR_INTF_WAIT:-60}\"\n457: +BOOT_WAIT=\"${CHR_BOOT_WAIT:-300}\"\n458: +INTFS=\"${CLAB_INTFS:-0}\"\n459: +STARTUP_CFG=/ftpboot/config.auto.rsc\n460: +# Per-container state in the rw layer: survives every restart, gone with the\n461: +# container (destroy, redeploy --cleanup).\n462: +DONE_MARKER=/.chr-launch-bootstrapped\n463: +READY_FILE=/.chr-launch-ready\n464: +ID_FILE=/.chr-launch-node-id\n465: +NICS_FILE=/.chr-launch-nics\n466: +MGMT_TABLE=mgmt\n467: +# every management object the launcher creates carries this comment, so each\n468: +# boot can find and re-apply them, and SaveConfig can leave them out\n469: +MGMT_COMMENT=clab-mgmt\n470: +MGMT_BR=br0\n471: +# eth0's addressing as last seen: a launcher re-run in the same network\n472: +# namespace finds eth0 already bridged and bare\n473: +MGMT_FILE=/.chr-launch-mgmt\n474: +SYSNET=/sys/class/net\n475: +\n476: +log() { echo \"chr-launch: $*\"; }\n477: +rm -f \"$READY_FILE\"\n478: +\n479: +FETCH_PORT=8080\n480: +\n481: +# ---- disk -----------------------------------------------------------------\n482: +IMG=\"\"\n483: +for f in /diskimage/*.img; do [ -f \"$f\" ] && IMG=\"$f\" && break; done\n484: +if [ -z \"$IMG\" ]; then\n485: +    log \"no /diskimage/*.img in this image: not a RouterOS CHR image\"\n486: +    exit 1\n487: +fi\n488: +# Only GROW the disk to the floor: an image already larger keeps its tail.\n489: +disk_bytes() {\n490: +    case \"$1\" in\n491: +        *G|*g) echo $(( ${1%[Gg]} * 1024 * 1024 * 1024 )) ;;\n492: +        *M|*m) echo $(( ${1%[Mm]} * 1024 * 1024 )) ;;\n493: +        *K|*k) echo $(( ${1%[Kk]} * 1024 )) ;;\n494: +        *) echo \"$1\" ;;\n495: +    esac\n496: +}\n497: +want_bytes=\"$(disk_bytes \"$DISK\")\"\n498: +cur_bytes=\"$(stat -c %s \"$IMG\" 2>/dev/null || wc -c < \"$IMG\")\"\n499: +if [ \"$cur_bytes\" -lt \"$want_bytes\" ] 2>/dev/null; then\n500: +    truncate -s \"$DISK\" \"$IMG\"\n501: +fi\n502: +\n503: +# ---- wait for containerlab's data interfaces ------------------------------\n504: +# shellcheck disable=SC2012  # interface names are ethN, ls is safe here\n505: +count_intfs() { ls -d \"$SYSNET\"/eth[1-9]* 2>/dev/null | wc -l; }\n506: +waited=0\n507: +while [ \"$(count_intfs)\" -lt \"$INTFS\" ] && [ \"$waited\" -lt \"$INTF_WAIT\" ]; do\n508: +    sleep 1\n509: +    waited=$((waited + 1))\n510: +done\n511: +log \"interfaces: $(count_intfs) of $INTFS expected after ${waited}s\"\n512: +\n513: +# ---- qemu command line ----------------------------------------------------\n514: +# The image's own qemu and firmware, as its /start.sh selects them.\n515: +case \"$(uname -m)\" in\n516: +    aarch64|arm64)\n517: +        QEMU=\"qemu-system-aarch64 -M virt -pflash /usr/share/qemu/edk2-aarch64-code.fd\"\n518: +        TCG_CPU=\"-cpu cortex-a57\" ;;\n519: +    *)\n520: +        QEMU=\"qemu-system-x86_64\"\n521: +        TCG_CPU=\"\" ;;\n522: +esac\n523: +if [ -e /dev/kvm ]; then\n524: +    ACCEL=\"-cpu host -accel kvm\"\n525: +else\n526: +    ACCEL=\"$TCG_CPU -accel tcg,split-wx=on\"\n527: +    log \"no /dev/kvm: running under TCG emulation (slow boot)\"\n528: +fi\n529: +\n530: +# MACs: unique per node AND per NIC. The container's eth0 MAC is unique per\n531: +# node, so its LAST TWO octets identify the node; the NIC's last byte is the\n532: +# interface index (00 = mgmt, 01 = ether2, …). Two nodes sharing a NIC MAC on\n533: +# a link drop each other's frames as their own. The id is read on the FIRST\n534: +# boot only and persisted (see Restart in the header).\n535: +node_id=\"$(cat \"$ID_FILE\" 2>/dev/null)\"\n536: +case \"$node_id\" in\n537: +    [0-9a-f][0-9a-f]:[0-9a-f][0-9a-f]) ;;\n538: +    *)\n539: +        e=\"$(cat \"$SYSNET/eth0/address\" 2>/dev/null)\"\n540: +        if [ -n \"$e\" ]; then\n541: +            o2=\"${e##*:}\"; rest_mac=\"${e%:*}\"; o1=\"${rest_mac##*:}\"\n542: +            node_id=\"$o1:$o2\"\n543: +        else\n544: +            node_id=\"$(hexdump -n2 -e '2/1 \"%02x:\"' /dev/urandom | sed 's/:$//')\"\n545: +        fi\n546: +        printf '%s\\n' \"$node_id\" > \"$ID_FILE\" 2>/dev/null \\\n547: +            || log \"node id $node_id not persisted: a restart will renumber the NICs\"\n548: +        ;;\n549: +esac\n550: +NET=\"-netdev tap,id=mgmt,ifname=tap0,script=no,downscript=no -device virtio-net,netdev=mgmt,mac=52:54:00:$node_id:00\"\n551: +\n552: +# ---- management: eth0 + tap0 on one bridge, eth0's addressing to the VM ----\n553: +ip_global() { # ip_global <4|6> <dev>: the first global address/prefix\n554: +    ip -\"$1\" addr show dev \"$2\" 2>/dev/null \\\n555: +        | sed -n '/scope global/s/^ *inet6\\{0,1\\} \\([^ ]*\\) .*/\\1/p' | head -n1\n556: +}\n557: +ip_gw() { # ip_gw <4|6>: the default gateway\n558: +    ip -\"$1\" route show default 2>/dev/null | sed -n 's/^default via \\([^ ]*\\) .*/\\1/p' | head -n1\n559: +}\n560: +M4=\"$(ip_global 4 eth0)\"; M6=\"$(ip_global 6 eth0)\"\n561: +G4=\"$(ip_gw 4)\"; G6=\"$(ip_gw 6)\"\n562: +if [ -n \"$M4$M6\" ]; then\n563: +    printf 'M4=%s\\nM6=%s\\nG4=%s\\nG6=%s\\n' \"$M4\" \"$M6\" \"$G4\" \"$G6\" > \"$MGMT_FILE\" 2>/dev/null || true\n564: +elif [ -f \"$MGMT_FILE\" ]; then\n565: +    # shellcheck disable=SC1090  # written by this script, four plain assignments\n566: +    . \"$MGMT_FILE\"\n567: +fi\n568: +log \"management: ${M4:-no IPv4}${M6:+, $M6} via ${G4:-no gateway}${G6:+, $G6}\"\n569: +\n570: +tunctl -t tap0 >/dev/null 2>&1 || ip tuntap add dev tap0 mode tap\n571: +if [ ! -e \"$SYSNET/$MGMT_BR\" ]; then\n572: +    _mtu=\"$(cat \"$SYSNET/eth0/mtu\" 2>/dev/null || echo 1500)\"\n573: +    ip addr flush dev eth0\n574: +    # The launcher's side of the bootstrap channel: IPv6 link-local on the\n575: +    # bridge only. eth0 and tap0 are plain bridge ports.\n576: +    sysctl -qw net.ipv6.conf.default.disable_ipv6=0 net.ipv6.conf.default.accept_ra=0 2>/dev/null\n577: +    brctl addbr \"$MGMT_BR\"\n578: +    brctl setfd \"$MGMT_BR\" 0 2>/dev/null\n579: +    echo 16384 > \"$SYSNET/$MGMT_BR/bridge/group_fwd_mask\" 2>/dev/null\n580: +    ip link set \"$MGMT_BR\" address \"52:54:01:$node_id:00\"\n581: +    ip link set tap0 mtu \"$_mtu\"\n582: +    ip link set \"$MGMT_BR\" mtu \"$_mtu\"\n583: +    # eth0 joins the bridge only once ether1 is configured (mgmt_attach): until\n584: +    # then nothing the router sends (its boot-time router advertisements, CHR's\n585: +    # DHCP discovers) reaches the management network.\n586: +    brctl addif \"$MGMT_BR\" tap0\n587: +    sysctl -qw net.ipv6.conf.eth0.disable_ipv6=1 net.ipv6.conf.tap0.disable_ipv6=1 \\\n588: +        \"net.ipv6.conf.$MGMT_BR.disable_ipv6=0\" \"net.ipv6.conf.$MGMT_BR.accept_ra=0\" \\\n589: +        \"net.ipv6.conf.$MGMT_BR.router_solicitations=0\" 2>/dev/null\n590: +fi\n591: +ip link set tap0 up\n592: +ip link set \"$MGMT_BR\" up\n593: +# RouterOS's ether1 link-local address, from the MAC this script gives it\n594: +VM_LL=\"fe80::5054:ff:fe${node_id%%:*}:${node_id##*:}00\"\n595: +VM_EP=\"$VM_LL%$MGMT_BR\"\n596: +# The bridge's own link-local address (the startup-config fetch source). Read\n597: +# once qemu runs: until then tap0, the bridge's only port, has no carrier and\n598: +# the address stays tentative.\n599: +LL=\"\"\n600: +bridge_ll() {\n601: +    _w=0\n602: +    while [ -z \"$LL\" ] && [ \"$_w\" -lt 20 ]; do\n603: +        LL=\"$(ip -6 addr show dev \"$MGMT_BR\" 2>/dev/null | grep -v tentative \\\n604: +            | sed -n 's/^ *inet6 \\(fe80::[^/]*\\)\\/64 .*/\\1/p' | head -n1)\"\n605: +        [ -n \"$LL\" ] || { sleep 1; _w=$((_w + 1)); }\n606: +    done\n607: +    [ -n \"$LL\" ] || log \"no IPv6 link-local on $MGMT_BR\"\n608: +}\n609: +\n610: +# NIC order = PCI order = the order of the -device args below. Walk\n611: +# eth1..ethMAX so a gap (links on ether2 and ether5 only) keeps RouterOS\n612: +# numbering aligned: a missing ethN still gets a tap, just no bridge. The count\n613: +# never shrinks across restarts: a `docker restart` recreates the netns and\n614: +# every lab veth of the node is gone, and a NIC absent for one boot loses the\n615: +# config bound to it. The tap keeps ether(N+1) a real interface, its link comes\n616: +# back when containerlab restores the veth or the lab is redeployed.\n617: +max=0\n618: +for d in \"$SYSNET\"/eth[1-9]*; do\n619: +    [ -e \"$d\" ] || continue\n620: +    n=\"${d##*/eth}\"\n621: +    [ \"$n\" -gt \"$max\" ] && max=\"$n\"\n622: +done\n623: +prev=\"$(cat \"$NICS_FILE\" 2>/dev/null)\"\n624: +case \"$prev\" in *[!0-9]*|\"\") prev=0 ;; esac\n625: +[ \"$prev\" -gt \"$max\" ] && max=\"$prev\"\n626: +# The wait above can end short of CLAB_INTFS on a large lab (links attach after\n627: +# every container started): the NIC set still spans the expected count, and\n628: +# the watcher bridges the late veths.\n629: +case \"$INTFS\" in *[!0-9]*|\"\") INTFS=0 ;; esac\n630: +[ \"$INTFS\" -gt \"$max\" ] && max=\"$INTFS\"\n631: +[ \"$max\" -gt \"$prev\" ] && { printf '%s\\n' \"$max\" > \"$NICS_FILE\" 2>/dev/null || true; }\n632: +\n633: +# RouterOS v7 memory ≈ 256 + 8·CPUS·(ifaces) MiB (MikroTik CHR system\n634: +# requirements). Scale by the HIGHEST generated NIC, not CLAB_INTFS: a sparse\n635: +# link on ether64 means CLAB_INTFS=1 yet 63 data NICs are created below.\n636: +if [ -z \"${MEM:-}\" ]; then\n637: +    MEM=$((256 + 8 * CPUS * max))\n638: +fi\n639: +\n640: +bridge_eth() { # bridge_eth <i>: container ethI joins brI beside tapI (brI made once)\n641: +    _mtu=\"$(cat \"$SYSNET/eth$1/mtu\" 2>/dev/null || echo 1500)\"\n642: +    if [ ! -e \"$SYSNET/br$1\" ]; then\n643: +        brctl addbr \"br$1\"\n644: +        ip link set \"br$1\" mtu \"$_mtu\"\n645: +        ip link set \"tap$1\" mtu \"$_mtu\"\n646: +        # forward LLDP (01:80:c2:00:00:0e, bit 14); STP/LACP cannot cross a bridge\n647: +        echo 16384 > \"$SYSNET/br$1/bridge/group_fwd_mask\" 2>/dev/null\n648: +        brctl addif \"br$1\" \"tap$1\"\n649: +    fi\n650: +    brctl addif \"br$1\" \"eth$1\"\n651: +    ip link set \"eth$1\" up\n652: +    ip link set \"br$1\" up\n653: +}\n654: +# A veth that lands or returns while qemu runs (containerlab restores a link\n655: +# right after a restart --node, a large lab attaches late, a peer node's\n656: +# redeploy recreates the pair) joins its bridge as it appears, so the RouterOS\n657: +# interface behind tapN gets its link back without a restart here.\n658: +watch_links() {\n659: +    _warned=\"\"\n660: +    while kill -0 \"$QEMU_PID\" 2>/dev/null; do\n661: +        i=1\n662: +        while [ \"$i\" -le \"$max\" ]; do\n663: +            if [ -e \"$SYSNET/eth$i\" ] && [ ! -e \"$SYSNET/br$i/brif/eth$i\" ]; then\n664: +                bridge_eth \"$i\" && log \"link eth$i attached: bridged to tap$i\"\n665: +            fi\n666: +            i=$((i + 1))\n667: +        done\n668: +        for d in \"$SYSNET\"/eth[1-9]*; do\n669: +            [ -e \"$d\" ] || continue\n670: +            n=\"${d##*/eth}\"\n671: +            [ \"$n\" -gt \"$max\" ] || continue\n672: +            case \" $_warned \" in *\" $n \"*) continue ;; esac\n673: +            _warned=\"$_warned $n\"\n674: +            log \"link eth$n attached after qemu started with $max data NICs: no RouterOS interface for it until the node is restarted\"\n675: +        done\n676: +        sleep 2\n677: +    done\n678: +}\n679: +\n680: +i=1\n681: +while [ \"$i\" -le \"$max\" ]; do\n682: +    tap=\"tap$i\"\n683: +    tunctl -t \"$tap\" >/dev/null 2>&1 || ip tuntap add dev \"$tap\" mode tap\n684: +    [ -e \"$SYSNET/eth$i\" ] && bridge_eth \"$i\"\n685: +    ip link set \"$tap\" up\n686: +    mac=\"$(printf '52:54:00:%s:%02x' \"$node_id\" \"$((i & 255))\")\"\n687: +    NET=\"$NET -netdev tap,id=d$i,ifname=$tap,script=no,downscript=no -device virtio-net,netdev=d$i,mac=$mac\"\n688: +    i=$((i + 1))\n689: +done\n690: +\n691: +# shellcheck disable=SC2086\n692: +$QEMU $ACCEL -display none -monitor none \\\n693: +    -m \"$MEM\" -smp \"cpus=$CPUS\" \\\n694: +    -drive \"file=$IMG,format=raw\" \\\n695: +    -serial telnet:0.0.0.0:5000,server,nowait \\\n696: +    $NET &\n697: +QEMU_PID=$!\n698: +trap 'kill \"$QEMU_PID\" 2>/dev/null; wait \"$QEMU_PID\" 2>/dev/null; exit 0' TERM INT\n699: +log \"qemu started (pid $QEMU_PID, mem ${MEM}M, cpus $CPUS, data nics $max)\"\n700: +watch_links &\n701: +\n702: +# ---- REST helpers ---------------------------------------------------------\n703: +json_str() { printf '%s' \"$1\" | sed 's/\\\\/\\\\\\\\/g; s/\"/\\\\\"/g'; }\n704: +rest() { # rest <user:pass> <path> [json]\n705: +    # Basic auth in a header, never in the URL: a password containing a URI\n706: +    # delimiter (#, ?, /) would change where the authority ends. REST_T is the\n707: +    # read timeout (default 20s).\n708: +    _auth=\"Authorization: Basic $(printf '%s' \"$1\" | base64 | tr -d '\\n')\"\n709: +    if [ $# -ge 3 ]; then\n710: +        wget -q -O- -T \"${REST_T:-20}\" --header \"Content-Type: application/json\" \\\n711: +            --header \"$_auth\" --post-data \"$3\" \"http://[$VM_EP]/rest/$2\" 2>/dev/null\n712: +    else\n713: +        wget -q -O- -T \"${REST_T:-20}\" --header \"$_auth\" \"http://[$VM_EP]/rest/$2\" 2>/dev/null\n714: +    fi\n715: +}\n716: +# busybox wget discards the body of an HTTP error response, and RouterOS puts\n717: +# its diagnosis ONLY there ({\"detail\":\"Script Error: … (line N column M)\"}; its\n718: +# /log stays silent). So the calls whose failure must be readable go over a raw\n719: +# HTTP POST through busybox nc. busybox nc exits the moment stdin hits EOF,\n720: +# reply or not, and RouterOS keeps the connection open despite\n721: +# `Connection: close`, so the request side is held open on a fifo and closed\n722: +# once Content-Length bytes of body have arrived.\n723: +resp_complete() { # resp_complete <file>: headers + Content-Length bytes of body arrived\n724: +    _cl=\"$(grep -i -m1 '^Content-Length:' \"$1\" 2>/dev/null | tr -dc '0-9')\"\n725: +    [ -n \"$_cl\" ] || return 1\n726: +    grep -q -m1 '^\\r*$' \"$1\" 2>/dev/null || return 1\n727: +    [ \"$(sed '1,/^\\r*$/d' \"$1\" | wc -c | tr -d ' ')\" -ge \"$_cl\" ]\n728: +}\n729: +rest_raw() { # rest_raw <user:pass> <path> <json> <timeout-s>  -> \"<status> <body>\"\n730: +    _f=\"/tmp/chr-rest.$$\"; rm -f \"$_f\" \"$_f.out\"; mkfifo \"$_f\" || return 1\n731: +    timeout \"$(( $4 + 5 ))\" nc \"$VM_EP\" 80 <\"$_f\" >\"$_f.out\" 2>/dev/null &\n732: +    _pid=$!\n733: +    exec 7>\"$_f\"\n734: +    printf 'POST /rest/%s HTTP/1.1\\r\\nHost: [%s]\\r\\nAuthorization: Basic %s\\r\\nContent-Type: application/json\\r\\nContent-Length: %s\\r\\nConnection: close\\r\\n\\r\\n%s' \\\n735: +        \"$2\" \"$VM_LL\" \"$(printf '%s' \"$1\" | base64 | tr -d '\\n')\" \\\n736: +        \"$(printf '%s' \"$3\" | wc -c | tr -d ' ')\" \"$3\" >&7\n737: +    _left=$4\n738: +    while kill -0 \"$_pid\" 2>/dev/null && ! resp_complete \"$_f.out\" && [ \"$_left\" -gt 0 ]; do\n739: +        sleep 1; _left=$(( _left - 1 ))\n740: +    done\n741: +    exec 7>&-\n742: +    wait \"$_pid\" 2>/dev/null\n743: +    _st=\"$(head -n1 \"$_f.out\" 2>/dev/null | tr -d '\\r' | cut -d' ' -f2)\"\n744: +    _body=\"$(sed '1,/^\\r*$/d' \"$_f.out\" 2>/dev/null | tr -d '\\r')\"\n745: +    rm -f \"$_f\" \"$_f.out\"\n746: +    printf '%s %s' \"${_st:-000}\" \"$_body\"\n747: +}\n748: +# RouterOS REST returns the object/array on success and an empty body for a\n749: +# bare `set`: only an explicit error object is a failure here. A silently\n750: +# failed call is caught by the read-back checks that follow each step.\n751: +is_err() { case \"$1\" in *'\"error\"'*) return 0 ;; *) return 1 ;; esac; }\n752: +\n753: +# ---- wait for RouterOS ----------------------------------------------------\n754: +waited=0\n755: +AUTH=\"\"\n756: +# A fresh official CHR disk has ONLY the factory `admin` account (blank\n757: +# password): the first boot authenticates as that account whatever --username\n758: +# asks for, and the requested user is created below. On a restart the pair that\n759: +# answers is the requested user.\n760: +while [ -z \"$AUTH\" ]; do\n761: +    for _cred in \"admin:\" \"admin:$PASSWORD\" \"$USERNAME:$PASSWORD\"; do\n762: +        rest \"$_cred\" system/resource >/dev/null 2>&1 && { AUTH=\"$_cred\"; break; }\n763: +    done\n764: +    [ -n \"$AUTH\" ] && break\n765: +    if ! kill -0 \"$QEMU_PID\" 2>/dev/null; then\n766: +        log \"qemu exited during boot\"\n767: +        exit 1\n768: +    fi\n769: +    sleep 2\n770: +    waited=$((waited + 2))\n771: +    if [ \"$waited\" -ge \"$BOOT_WAIT\" ]; then\n772: +        log \"RouterOS did not answer within ${BOOT_WAIT}s: leaving qemu running\"\n773: +        wait \"$QEMU_PID\"\n774: +        exit 1\n775: +    fi\n776: +done\n777: +log \"RouterOS answered after ${waited}s\"\n778: +\n779: +# Ready means a user can log in: RouterOS starts its SSH server well after REST\n780: +# answers on a slow boot (about 40 s later under TCG on arm64), so when the\n781: +# configuration keeps SSH enabled, its port must send the banner first.\n782: +# A config that disables SSH is not waited for; a banner that never comes is\n783: +# logged and the node is still declared ready (REST works).\n784: +ssh_banner() { (sleep 3) | nc \"$VM_EP\" 22 2>/dev/null | head -c 7; }\n785: +ready() {\n786: +    case \"$(rest \"$AUTH\" \"ip/service?name=ssh\")\" in\n787: +        *'\"disabled\":\"false\"'*)\n788: +            _t0=$(date +%s)\n789: +            until [ \"$(ssh_banner)\" = \"SSH-2.0\" ]; do\n790: +                if [ $(( $(date +%s) - _t0 )) -ge \"$BOOT_WAIT\" ]; then\n791: +                    log \"SSH did not answer within $(( $(date +%s) - _t0 ))s (REST works)\"\n792: +                    break\n793: +                fi\n794: +                sleep 2\n795: +            done\n796: +            ;;\n797: +    esac\n798: +    touch \"$READY_FILE\" 2>/dev/null || true\n799: +    log \"RouterOS ready\"\n800: +}\n801: +\n802: +# A failed step WITHHOLDS the ready marker (qemu keeps running, so the node can\n803: +# still be inspected over the serial console, port 5000 in the container).\n804: +bootstrap_fail() {\n805: +    log \"credential bootstrap FAILED: $1\"\n806: +    wait \"$QEMU_PID\"\n807: +    exit 0\n808: +}\n809: +\n810: +# ---- management: ether1 addressing and its own routing table ----------------\n811: +# ether1 gets the container's management addresses, and their return path lives\n812: +# in a `mgmt` routing table selected by a rule on those source addresses: a\n813: +# default route the lab adds to `main` (a static 0.0.0.0/0, a PPPoE client's\n814: +# default route, a dual-WAN pair) would otherwise take SSH/REST replies away\n815: +# from the management network while the data plane works. RouterOS CHR\n+---\n+# -{{ kind_display_name }}-\n+\n+[MikroTik RouterOS](https://mikrotik.com/software) Cloud Hosted Router (CHR) from the **official [`mikrotik/chr`](https://hub.docker.com/r/mikrotik/chr) image** is identified with the `-{{ kind_code_name }}-` kind in the [topology file](../topo-def-file.md). The image is published by MikroTik for `amd64` and `arm64` and is used as is: no [vrnetlab](../vrnetlab.md) build is needed.\n+\n+The image is a QEMU VM (Alpine, qemu and the CHR disk) in a container. Its own `/start.sh` is not made for orchestrated links ([#3116](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/issues/3116)), so the kind writes a launcher script into the node's lab directory, mounts it read-only and runs it as the entrypoint instead. The launcher uses only the tools that ship in the image.\n+\n+/// note\n+The [`mikrotik_ros`](vr-ros.md) kind runs CHR from an image you build with vrnetlab. Both kinds use the same interface naming and startup config format, so a lab moves from one to the other by changing `kind` and `image`.\n+///\n+\n+## Getting -{{ kind_display_name }}- image\n+\n+```bash\n+docker pull mikrotik/chr:7.24.5\n+```\n+\n+Tags follow RouterOS versions, plus `stable`, `long-term`, `testing` and `development`. This kind has been tested with 7.24.5 (stable), 7.23.8 (long-term) and 7.25rc2 (testing).\n+\n+## Managing -{{ kind_display_name }}- nodes\n+\n+/// tab | CLI\n+```bash\n+ssh admin@<node-name>\n+```\n+///\n+/// tab | REST API\n+```bash\n+curl -u admin:admin http://<node-name>/rest/system/resource\n+```\n+///\n+/// tab | Winbox / API\n+Winbox (TCP 8291) and the RouterOS API (TCP 8728, 8729 for TLS) are reachable on the node's management address.\n+///\n+/// tab | Console\n+The serial console is on port 5000 inside the container:\n+```bash\n+docker exec -it <node-name> nc 127.0.0.1 5000\n+```\n+///\n+/// tab | bash\n+```bash\n+docker exec -it <node-name> sh\n+```\n+///\n+\n+The node's management address is RouterOS's own `ether1` address ([transparent management](#management-connectivity)), so every service RouterOS runs answers on it directly, with nothing forwarded.\n+\n+/// note\n+Default credentials are `admin:admin`. With custom [`credentials`](../nodes.md#credentials) the kind creates that user in the `full` group and disables the factory `admin` account, which has a blank password on a fresh CHR disk.\n+///\n+\n+## Interface naming\n+\n+You can use [interface names](../topo-def-file.md#interface-naming) in the topology file like they appear in RouterOS: `etherX`, where `X` is the port number.\n+\n+* `ether2` - first data port\n+* `ether3` - second data port, and so on\n+\n+/// admonition\n+    type: warning\n+Data ports start at `ether2`: `ether1` is the management interface. Using `ether1` in a topology results in an error.\n+///\n+\n+The container interface `ethN` is RouterOS `ether(N+1)`, also when links leave gaps: a link on `eth12` alone still lands on `ether13`, with `ether2` to `ether12` present and unlinked. Interface MTU follows the link MTU, and LLDP frames are forwarded, so `/ip neighbor` shows the peers.\n+\n+## Node configuration\n+\n+-{{ kind_display_name }}- nodes boot with the default CHR configuration plus the credentials, the identity (set to the node name) and the management setup described below.\n+\n+### Startup configuration\n+\n+A RouterOS script (`.rsc`) set with [`startup-config`](../nodes.md#startup-config) is imported once the router is up:\n+\n+```yaml\n+name: chr_lab\n+topology:\n+  nodes:\n+    r1:\n+      kind: mikrotik_chr\n+      image: mikrotik/chr:7.24.5\n+      startup-config: r1.rsc\n+```\n+\n+The file is copied to the node's lab directory as `ftpboot/config.auto.rsc` and imported over the REST API. RouterOS imports line by line and stops at the first error: in that case the container log carries RouterOS's own reason with the line number, for example\n+\n+```\n+chr-launch: startup config import FAILED: Script Error: bad parameter network-type (line 3 column 59); lines before the failing one were applied, the rest were not\n+```\n+\n+and the node does not become ready.\n+\n+### Saving configuration\n+\n+[`containerlab save`](../../cmd/save.md) writes each node's `/export` to `ftpboot/config.auto.rsc` in its lab directory. The management objects the launcher creates at every boot (everything tagged `clab-mgmt`, and the `ether1` DHCP client) are left out, so the saved file can be used as the startup config of the next deployment, also one with different management addresses.\n+\n+### Node readiness\n+\n+The node is ready once RouterOS accepted the credentials and the startup config, reaches its gateway from the management address and, unless the configuration disables SSH, its SSH server answers. An SSH server that does not answer within `CHR_BOOT_WAIT` is logged, and the node is still declared ready, since REST and the console work. The container log then shows\n+\n+```\n+chr-launch: RouterOS ready\n+```\n+\n+and the kind's default [healthcheck](../nodes.md#healthcheck) reports the container `healthy`, so [`stages`](../nodes.md#stages) with `wait-for` can depend on it. A node whose startup config was rejected never becomes healthy. A `healthcheck` set in the topology replaces the default one.\n+\n+### Resources\n+\n+The kind reads the official image's own environment variables:\n+\n+| Variable | Default | Purpose |\n+|----------|---------|---------|\n+| `MEM` | 256 MiB + 8 MiB per data interface and vCPU | VM memory (a QEMU `-m` value, e.g. `1024` or `1G`) |\n+| `CPUS` | `1` | VM vCPUs |\n+| `DISK` | `128M` | minimum disk size (the disk only grows) |\n+| `CHR_INTF_WAIT` | `60` | seconds to wait for the data interfaces containerlab attaches before the VM starts |\n+| `CHR_BOOT_WAIT` | `300` | seconds to wait for RouterOS to answer, for the startup config import, and for SSH before the node is declared ready |\n+\n+```yaml\n+r1:\n+  kind: mikrotik_chr\n+  image: mikrotik/chr:7.24.5\n+  env:\n+    MEM: 1024\n+    CPUS: 2\n+```\n+\n+KVM is used when `/dev/kvm` is available. Without it the image's QEMU runs under TCG emulation: the node works, but boots several times slower.\n+\n+## Management connectivity\n+\n+Management is transparent: the IPv4 and IPv6 addresses and gateways containerlab gives the node's `eth0` are configured on RouterOS `ether1`, and the container's `eth0` is bridged to the VM's `ether1`. The router is the node's management address: SSH, the REST API, Winbox, SNMP and every other service answer on it, LLDP/MNDP work on the management network, and the address in the topology table is the one in the RouterOS configuration.\n+\n+The image ships busybox only (no `tc`), so where vrnetlab's [transparent management](../vrnetlab.md#management-interface) redirects with tc-mirred, this kind uses a Linux bridge. The container itself has no address on the management network; it reaches RouterOS for bootstrap over IPv6 link-local on that bridge.\n+\n+The management return path lives in its own routing table, `mgmt`, selected by routing rules for the management addresses, and CHR's `ether1` DHCP client is disabled. A default route in the lab configuration, a PPPoE client's default route or a dual-WAN setup in `main` therefore does not cut SSH or REST access to the node. RouterOS's router advertisements on `ether1` carry a zero router lifetime, so no host on the management network takes the router as its IPv6 default gateway; the container joins the management network only once this is in place. The objects carry the comment `clab-mgmt` and are re-applied at boot when the container's addressing changed.\n+\n+/// warning\n+Do not remove or change the `clab-mgmt` objects or the `mgmt` routing table: management of the node depends on them.\n+///\n+\n+## Restarts\n+\n+[`containerlab restart`](../../cmd/restart.md) and a `/system reboot` inside RouterOS keep the interface names, MAC addresses, configuration and links: the launcher persists the NIC identity in the container on first boot and does not re-run the bootstrap. A plain `docker restart` keeps the router and its management, but Docker deletes the node's veth links, which stay down until the node is restarted with containerlab or the lab is redeployed.\n+\n+## File mounts\n+\n+For each node containerlab creates in its [lab directory](../conf-artifacts.md):\n+\n+* `ftpboot/` - mounted to `/ftpboot`, holds `config.auto.rsc`\n+* `chr-launch.sh` - the launcher, mounted read-only to `/chr-launch.sh` and rewritten on every deploy\ndiff --git a/mkdocs.yml b/mkdocs.yml\nindex 914ad86..d6273e0 100644\n--- a/mkdocs.yml\n+++ b/mkdocs.yml\n@@ -46,10 +46,11 @@ nav:\n           - FTOS10v: manual/kinds/vr-ftosv.md\n           - Enterprise SONiC: manual/kinds/dell_sonic.md\n       - PLVision:\n           - Enterprise SONiC: manual/kinds/plvision_sonic.md\n       - MikroTik RouterOS: manual/kinds/vr-ros.md\n+      - MikroTik RouterOS CHR (official image): manual/kinds/mikrotik_chr.md\n       - Huawei VRP: manual/kinds/huawei_vrp.md\n       - IPInfusion OcNOS: manual/kinds/ipinfusion-ocnos.md\n       - VyOS Networks VyOS: manual/kinds/vyosnetworks_vyos.md\n       - OpenBSD: manual/kinds/openbsd.md\n       - FreeBSD: manual/kinds/freebsd.md\ndiff --git a/nodes/mikrotik_chr/chr-launch.sh b/nodes/mikrotik_chr/chr-launch.sh\nnew file mode 100644\nindex 0000000..aa039f4\n--- /dev/null\n+++ b/nodes/mikrotik_chr/chr-launch.sh\n@@ -0,0 +1,670 @@\n+#!/bin/sh\n+# chr-launch.sh: run the OFFICIAL mikrotik/chr image (MikroTik RouterOS Cloud\n+# Hosted Router) as a containerlab node, image untouched.\n+#\n+# The image is MikroTik's own Docker Hub distribution (Alpine + qemu +\n+# /diskimage/chr-<ver>.img + /start.sh). This script is bind-mounted into the\n+# node and set as its entrypoint by the containerlab `mikrotik_chr` kind. It replaces\n+# the image's /start.sh, which is not orchestration-ready (containerlab#3116):\n+# it scans interfaces once before containerlab attaches the links, bridges the\n+# management NIC so the container IP never reaches the router, and imports no\n+# startup config.\n+#\n+# What it does (vrnetlab's mikrotik/routeros launch.py is the model, re-done\n+# with the image's own busybox + qemu: no python, no ftp client, no tc):\n+#   1. waits for the CLAB_INTFS data interfaces containerlab attaches (eth1..N)\n+#   2. management, transparent: the container's eth0 is bridged with the VM's\n+#      ether1 tap, and eth0's addresses and default gateways move into RouterOS\n+#      ether1. The router IS the node's management address: every service and\n+#      LLDP reach it directly, nothing is port-forwarded. The image ships only\n+#      busybox (no tc), so this uses a bridge where vrnetlab uses tc-mirred\n+#   3. data: one tap per container interface, bridged pairwise (ethN <-> tapN,\n+#      LLDP forwarded). NIC order in the VM follows the eth index, so container\n+#      ethN is always RouterOS ether(N+1), even with gaps\n+#   4. after boot, over IPv6 link-local on the management bridge (no address is\n+#      added to the management network): sets the credentials and the identity,\n+#      configures ether1 with the management addresses and keeps their return\n+#      path in its own routing table (`mgmt`, below), then fetches\n+#      /ftpboot/config.auto.rsc (the containerlab startup-config, when present)\n+#      from the launcher and `/import`s it\n+#   5. prints the ready marker on the container log and waits on qemu\n+#\n+# Restart (docker restart, containerlab restart --node): RouterOS keys an\n+# ethernet interface on its MAC. A NIC that comes back with a new MAC is a NEW\n+# device (ether1 becomes ether3) and the DHCP client bound to ether1 never\n+# binds. Docker re-rolls the container's eth0 MAC whenever the netns is\n+# recreated, so the node id that seeds every NIC MAC and the data NIC count are\n+# persisted in the container on the first boot and reused: the restarted qemu\n+# carries the same NICs in the same order and RouterOS keeps ether1..N and\n+# their config. The REST bootstrap is skipped on a restart; the management\n+# step re-runs and re-applies the container's current addressing.\n+#\n+# Architectures: the image's own qemu (qemu-system-x86_64 on amd64,\n+#   qemu-system-aarch64 + edk2 on arm64), KVM when /dev/kvm exists, else TCG.\n+# Args (the kind's Cmd): --hostname H [--username U --password P]\n+#   (unknown flags are ignored); credentials default to the USERNAME/PASSWORD env\n+# Env (all optional, set under the node's `env:`); MEM, CPUS and DISK are the\n+#   official image's own variables:\n+#   MEM (qemu -m value, default 256 MiB + 8 MiB per data NIC and vCPU), CPUS (default 1),\n+#   DISK (default 128M), CHR_INTF_WAIT (s, 60), CHR_BOOT_WAIT (s, 300)\n+# Serial console: port 5000 inside the container\n+#   (docker exec -it <node> nc 127.0.0.1 5000); the container itself has no\n+#   address on the management network.\n+# Ready: /.chr-launch-ready exists once RouterOS is ready (the kind's default\n+#   healthcheck); it is removed at every start.\n+# Log lines (docker logs clab-<lab>-<node>), all prefixed \"chr-launch:\":\n+#   \"RouterOS ready\"                   login + startup config done\n+#   \"startup config import FAILED: …\"  the router rejected the .rsc (RouterOS's\n+#                                      own reason, with the line number)\n+#   \"credential bootstrap FAILED: …\"   the user/password step failed\n+#   \"management setup FAILED: …\"       the ether1 address / mgmt table step failed\n+#\n+# Copyright 2026 NetPilot Labs\n+# Licensed under the BSD 3-Clause License.\n+# SPDX-License-Identifier: BSD-3-Clause\n+\n+set -u\n+\n+# Credentials come from the USERNAME/PASSWORD env the kind sets (no quoting\n+# limits on the password); --username/--password still override them.\n+USERNAME=\"${USERNAME:-admin}\"\n+PASSWORD=\"${PASSWORD-admin}\"\n+HOSTNAME_ARG=\"\"\n+while [ $# -gt 0 ]; do\n+    case \"$1\" in\n+        --username) USERNAME=\"$2\"; shift 2 ;;\n+        --password) PASSWORD=\"$2\"; shift 2 ;;\n+        --hostname) HOSTNAME_ARG=\"$2\"; shift 2 ;;\n+        --connection-mode) shift 2 ;;\n+        --trace) shift ;;\n+        *) shift ;;\n+    esac\n+done\n+[ -n \"$HOSTNAME_ARG\" ] || HOSTNAME_ARG=\"$(hostname)\"\n+\n+CPUS=\"${CPUS:-1}\"\n+DISK=\"${DISK:-128M}\"\n+INTF_WAIT=\"${CHR_INTF_WAIT:-60}\"\n+BOOT_WAIT=\"${CHR_BOOT_WAIT:-300}\"\n+INTFS=\"${CLAB_INTFS:-0}\"\n+STARTUP_CFG=/ftpboot/config.auto.rsc\n+# Per-container state in the rw layer: survives every restart, gone with the\n+# container (destroy, redeploy --cleanup).\n+DONE_MARKER=/.chr-launch-bootstrapped\n+READY_FILE=/.chr-launch-ready\n+ID_FILE=/.chr-launch-node-id\n+NICS_FILE=/.chr-launch-nics\n+MGMT_TABLE=mgmt\n+# every management object the launcher creates carries this comment, so each\n+# boot can find and re-apply them, and SaveConfig can leave them out\n+MGMT_COMMENT=clab-mgmt\n+MGMT_BR=br0\n+# eth0's addressing as last seen: a launcher re-run in the same network\n+# namespace finds eth0 already bridged and bare\n+MGMT_FILE=/.chr-launch-mgmt\n+SYSNET=/sys/class/net\n+\n+log() { echo \"chr-launch: $*\"; }\n+rm -f \"$READY_FILE\"\n+\n+FETCH_PORT=8080\n+\n+# ---- disk -----------------------------------------------------------------\n+IMG=\"\"\n+for f in /diskimage/*.img; do [ -f \"$f\" ] && IMG=\"$f\" && break; done\n+if [ -z \"$IMG\" ]; then\n+    log \"no /diskimage/*.img in this image: not a RouterOS CHR image\"\n+    exit 1\n+fi\n+# Only GROW the disk to the floor: an image already larger keeps its tail.\n+disk_bytes() {\n+    case \"$1\" in\n+        *G|*g) echo $(( ${1%[Gg]} * 1024 * 1024 * 1024 )) ;;\n+        *M|*m) echo $(( ${1%[Mm]} * 1024 * 1024 )) ;;\n+        *K|*k) echo $(( ${1%[Kk]} * 1024 )) ;;\n+        *) echo \"$1\" ;;\n+    esac\n+}\n+want_bytes=\"$(disk_bytes \"$DISK\")\"\n+cur_bytes=\"$(stat -c %s \"$IMG\" 2>/dev/null || wc -c < \"$IMG\")\"\n+if [ \"$cur_bytes\" -lt \"$want_bytes\" ] 2>/dev/null; then\n+    truncate -s \"$DISK\" \"$IMG\"\n+fi\n+\n+# ---- wait for containerlab's data interfaces ------------------------------\n+# shellcheck disable=SC2012  # interface names are ethN, ls is safe here\n+count_intfs() { ls -d \"$SYSNET\"/eth[1-9]* 2>/dev/null | wc -l; }\n+waited=0\n+while [ \"$(count_intfs)\" -lt \"$INTFS\" ] && [ \"$waited\" -lt \"$INTF_WAIT\" ]; do\n+    sleep 1\n+    waited=$((waited + 1))\n+done\n+log \"interfaces: $(count_intfs) of $INTFS expected after ${waited}s\"\n+\n+# ---- qemu command line ----------------------------------------------------\n+# The image's own qemu and firmware, as its /start.sh selects them.\n+case \"$(uname -m)\" in\n+    aarch64|arm64)\n+        QEMU=\"qemu-system-aarch64 -M virt -pflash /usr/share/qemu/edk2-aarch64-code.fd\"\n+        TCG_CPU=\"-cpu cortex-a57\" ;;\n+    *)\n+        QEMU=\"qemu-system-x86_64\"\n+        TCG_CPU=\"\" ;;\n+esac\n+if [ -e /dev/kvm ]; then\n+    ACCEL=\"-cpu host -accel kvm\"\n+else\n+    ACCEL=\"$TCG_CPU -accel tcg,split-wx=on\"\n+    log \"no /dev/kvm: running under TCG emulation (slow boot)\"\n+fi\n+\n+# MACs: unique per node AND per NIC. The container's eth0 MAC is unique per\n+# node, so its LAST TWO octets identify the node; the NIC's last byte is the\n+# interface index (00 = mgmt, 01 = ether2, …). Two nodes sharing a NIC MAC on\n+# a link drop each other's frames as their own. The id is read on the FIRST\n+# boot only and persisted (see Restart in the header).\n+node_id=\"$(cat \"$ID_FILE\" 2>/dev/null)\"\n+case \"$node_id\" in\n+    [0-9a-f][0-9a-f]:[0-9a-f][0-9a-f]) ;;\n+    *)\n+        e=\"$(cat \"$SYSNET/eth0/address\" 2>/dev/null)\"\n+        if [ -n \"$e\" ]; then\n+            o2=\"${e##*:}\"; rest_mac=\"${e%:*}\"; o1=\"${rest_mac##*:}\"\n+            node_id=\"$o1:$o2\"\n+        else\n+            node_id=\"$(hexdump -n2 -e '2/1 \"%02x:\"' /dev/urandom | sed 's/:$//')\"\n+        fi\n+        printf '%s\\n' \"$node_id\" > \"$ID_FILE\" 2>/dev/null \\\n+            || log \"node id $node_id not persisted: a restart will renumber the NICs\"\n+        ;;\n+esac\n+NET=\"-netdev tap,id=mgmt,ifname=tap0,script=no,downscript=no -device virtio-net,netdev=mgmt,mac=52:54:00:$node_id:00\"\n+\n+# ---- management: eth0 + tap0 on one bridge, eth0's addressing to the VM ----\n+ip_global() { # ip_global <4|6> <dev>: the first global address/prefix\n+    ip -\"$1\" addr show dev \"$2\" 2>/dev/null \\\n+        | sed -n '/scope global/s/^ *inet6\\{0,1\\} \\([^ ]*\\) .*/\\1/p' | head -n1\n+}\n+ip_gw() { # ip_gw <4|6>: the default gateway\n+    ip -\"$1\" route show default 2>/dev/null | sed -n 's/^default via \\([^ ]*\\) .*/\\1/p' | head -n1\n+}\n+M4=\"$(ip_global 4 eth0)\"; M6=\"$(ip_global 6 eth0)\"\n+G4=\"$(ip_gw 4)\"; G6=\"$(ip_gw 6)\"\n+if [ -n \"$M4$M6\" ]; then\n+    printf 'M4=%s\\nM6=%s\\nG4=%s\\nG6=%s\\n' \"$M4\" \"$M6\" \"$G4\" \"$G6\" > \"$MGMT_FILE\" 2>/dev/null || true\n+elif [ -f \"$MGMT_FILE\" ]; then\n+    # shellcheck disable=SC1090  # written by this script, four plain assignments\n+    . \"$MGMT_FILE\"\n+fi\n+log \"management: ${M4:-no IPv4}${M6:+, $M6} via ${G4:-no gateway}${G6:+, $G6}\"\n+\n+tunctl -t tap0 >/dev/null 2>&1 || ip tuntap add dev tap0 mode tap\n+if [ ! -e \"$SYSNET/$MGMT_BR\" ]; then\n+    _mtu=\"$(cat \"$SYSNET/eth0/mtu\" 2>/dev/null || echo 1500)\"\n+    ip addr flush dev eth0\n+    # The launcher's side of the bootstrap channel: IPv6 link-local on the\n+    # bridge only. eth0 and tap0 are plain bridge ports.\n+    sysctl -qw net.ipv6.conf.default.disable_ipv6=0 net.ipv6.conf.default.accept_ra=0 2>/dev/null\n+    brctl addbr \"$MGMT_BR\"\n+    brctl setfd \"$MGMT_BR\" 0 2>/dev/null\n+    echo 16384 > \"$SYSNET/$MGMT_BR/bridge/group_fwd_mask\" 2>/dev/null\n+    ip link set \"$MGMT_BR\" address \"52:54:01:$node_id:00\"\n+    ip link set tap0 mtu \"$_mtu\"\n+    ip link set \"$MGMT_BR\" mtu \"$_mtu\"\n+    # eth0 joins the bridge only once ether1 is configured (mgmt_attach): until\n+    # then nothing the router sends (its boot-time router advertisements, CHR's\n+    # DHCP discovers) reaches the management network.\n+    brctl addif \"$MGMT_BR\" tap0\n+    sysctl -qw net.ipv6.conf.eth0.disable_ipv6=1 net.ipv6.conf.tap0.disable_ipv6=1 \\\n+        \"net.ipv6.conf.$MGMT_BR.disable_ipv6=0\" \"net.ipv6.conf.$MGMT_BR.accept_ra=0\" \\\n+        \"net.ipv6.conf.$MGMT_BR.router_solicitations=0\" 2>/dev/null\n+fi\n+ip link set tap0 up\n+ip link set \"$MGMT_BR\" up\n+# RouterOS's ether1 link-local address, from the MAC this script gives it\n+VM_LL=\"fe80::5054:ff:fe${node_id%%:*}:${node_id##*:}00\"\n+VM_EP=\"$VM_LL%$MGMT_BR\"\n+# The bridge's own link-local address (the startup-config fetch source). Read\n+# once qemu runs: until then tap0, the bridge's only port, has no carrier and\n+# the address stays tentative.\n+LL=\"\"\n+bridge_ll() {\n+    _w=0\n+    while [ -z \"$LL\" ] && [ \"$_w\" -lt 20 ]; do\n+        LL=\"$(ip -6 addr show dev \"$MGMT_BR\" 2>/dev/null | grep -v tentative \\\n+            | sed -n 's/^ *inet6 \\(fe80::[^/]*\\)\\/64 .*/\\1/p' | head -n1)\"\n+        [ -n \"$LL\" ] || { sleep 1; _w=$((_w + 1)); }\n+    done\n+    [ -n \"$LL\" ] || log \"no IPv6 link-local on $MGMT_BR\"\n+}\n+\n+# NIC order = PCI order = the order of the -device args below. Walk\n+# eth1..ethMAX so a gap (links on ether2 and ether5 only) keeps RouterOS\n+# numbering aligned: a missing ethN still gets a tap, just no bridge. The count\n+# never shrinks across restarts: a `docker restart` recreates the netns and\n+# every lab veth of the node is gone, and a NIC absent for one boot loses the\n+# config bound to it. The tap keeps ether(N+1) a real interface, its link comes\n+# back when containerlab restores the veth or the lab is redeployed.\n+max=0\n+for d in \"$SYSNET\"/eth[1-9]*; do\n+    [ -e \"$d\" ] || continue\n+    n=\"${d##*/eth}\"\n+    [ \"$n\" -gt \"$max\" ] && max=\"$n\"\n+done\n+prev=\"$(cat \"$NICS_FILE\" 2>/dev/null)\"\n+case \"$prev\" in *[!0-9]*|\"\") prev=0 ;; esac\n+[ \"$prev\" -gt \"$max\" ] && max=\"$prev\"\n+# The wait above can end short of CLAB_INTFS on a large lab (links attach after\n+# every container started): the NIC set still spans the expected count, and\n+# the watcher bridges the late veths.\n+case \"$INTFS\" in *[!0-9]*|\"\") INTFS=0 ;; esac\n+[ \"$INTFS\" -gt \"$max\" ] && max=\"$INTFS\"\n+[ \"$max\" -gt \"$prev\" ] && { printf '%s\\n' \"$max\" > \"$NICS_FILE\" 2>/dev/null || true; }\n+\n+# RouterOS v7 memory ≈ 256 + 8·CPUS·(ifaces) MiB (MikroTik CHR system\n+# requirements). Scale by the HIGHEST generated NIC, not CLAB_INTFS: a sparse\n+# link on ether64 means CLAB_INTFS=1 yet 63 data NICs are created below.\n+if [ -z \"${MEM:-}\" ]; then\n+    MEM=$((256 + 8 * CPUS * max))\n+fi\n+\n+bridge_eth() { # bridge_eth <i>: container ethI joins brI beside tapI (brI made once)\n+    _mtu=\"$(cat \"$SYSNET/eth$1/mtu\" 2>/dev/null || echo 1500)\"\n+    if [ ! -e \"$SYSNET/br$1\" ]; then\n+        brctl addbr \"br$1\"\n+        ip link set \"br$1\" mtu \"$_mtu\"\n+        ip link set \"tap$1\" mtu \"$_mtu\"\n+        # forward LLDP (01:80:c2:00:00:0e, bit 14); STP/LACP cannot cross a bridge\n+        echo 16384 > \"$SYSNET/br$1/bridge/group_fwd_mask\" 2>/dev/null\n+        brctl addif \"br$1\" \"tap$1\"\n+    fi\n+    brctl addif \"br$1\" \"eth$1\"\n+    ip link set \"eth$1\" up\n+    ip link set \"br$1\" up\n+}\n+# A veth that lands or returns while qemu runs (containerlab restores a link\n+# right after a restart --node, a large lab attaches late, a peer node's\n+# redeploy recreates the pair) joins its bridge as it appears, so the RouterOS\n+# interface behind tapN gets its link back without a restart here.\n+watch_links() {\n+    _warned=\"\"\n+    while kill -0 \"$QEMU_PID\" 2>/dev/null; do\n+        i=1\n+        while [ \"$i\" -le \"$max\" ]; do\n+            if [ -e \"$SYSNET/eth$i\" ] && [ ! -e \"$SYSNET/br$i/brif/eth$i\" ]; then\n+                bridge_eth \"$i\" && log \"link eth$i attached: bridged to tap$i\"\n+            fi\n+            i=$((i + 1))\n+        done\n+        for d in \"$SYSNET\"/eth[1-9]*; do\n+            [ -e \"$d\" ] || continue\n+            n=\"${d##*/eth}\"\n+            [ \"$n\" -gt \"$max\" ] || continue\n+            case \" $_warned \" in *\" $n \"*) continue ;; esac\n+            _warned=\"$_warned $n\"\n+            log \"link eth$n attached after qemu started with $max data NICs: no RouterOS interface for it until the node is restarted\"\n+        done\n+        sleep 2\n+    done\n+}\n+\n+i=1\n+while [ \"$i\" -le \"$max\" ]; do\n+    tap=\"tap$i\"\n+    tunctl -t \"$tap\" >/dev/null 2>&1 || ip tuntap add dev \"$tap\" mode tap\n+    [ -e \"$SYSNET/eth$i\" ] && bridge_eth \"$i\"\n+    ip link set \"$tap\" up\n+    mac=\"$(printf '52:54:00:%s:%02x' \"$node_id\" \"$((i & 255))\")\"\n+    NET=\"$NET -netdev tap,id=d$i,ifname=$tap,script=no,downscript=no -device virtio-net,netdev=d$i,mac=$mac\"\n+    i=$((i + 1))\n+done\n+\n+# shellcheck disable=SC2086\n+$QEMU $ACCEL -display none -monitor none \\\n+    -m \"$MEM\" -smp \"cpus=$CPUS\" \\\n+    -drive \"file=$IMG,format=raw\" \\\n+    -serial telnet:0.0.0.0:5000,server,nowait \\\n+    $NET &\n+QEMU_PID=$!\n+trap 'kill \"$QEMU_PID\" 2>/dev/null; wait \"$QEMU_PID\" 2>/dev/null; exit 0' TERM INT\n+log \"qemu started (pid $QEMU_PID, mem ${MEM}M, cpus $CPUS, data nics $max)\"\n+watch_links &\n+\n+# ---- REST helpers ---------------------------------------------------------\n+json_str() { printf '%s' \"$1\" | sed 's/\\\\/\\\\\\\\/g; s/\"/\\\\\"/g'; }\n+rest() { # rest <user:pass> <path> [json]\n+    # Basic auth in a header, never in the URL: a password containing a URI\n+    # delimiter (#, ?, /) would change where the authority ends. REST_T is the\n+    # read timeout (default 20s).\n+    _auth=\"Authorization: Basic $(printf '%s' \"$1\" | base64 | tr -d '\\n')\"\n+    if [ $# -ge 3 ]; then\n+        wget -q -O- -T \"${REST_T:-20}\" --header \"Content-Type: application/json\" \\\n+            --header \"$_auth\" --post-data \"$3\" \"http://[$VM_EP]/rest/$2\" 2>/dev/null\n+    else\n+        wget -q -O- -T \"${REST_T:-20}\" --header \"$_auth\" \"http://[$VM_EP]/rest/$2\" 2>/dev/null\n+    fi\n+}\n+# busybox wget discards the body of an HTTP error response, and RouterOS puts\n+# its diagnosis ONLY there ({\"detail\":\"Script Error: … (line N column M)\"}; its\n+# /log stays silent). So the calls whose failure must be readable go over a raw\n+# HTTP POST through busybox nc. busybox nc exits the moment stdin hits EOF,\n+# reply or not, and RouterOS keeps the connection open despite\n+# `Connection: close`, so the request side is held open on a fifo and closed\n+# once Content-Length bytes of body have arrived.\n+resp_complete() { # resp_complete <file>: headers + Content-Length bytes of body arrived\n+    _cl=\"$(grep -i -m1 '^Content-Length:' \"$1\" 2>/dev/null | tr -dc '0-9')\"\n+    [ -n \"$_cl\" ] || return 1\n+    grep -q -m1 '^\\r*$' \"$1\" 2>/dev/null || return 1\n+    [ \"$(sed '1,/^\\r*$/d' \"$1\" | wc -c | tr -d ' ')\" -ge \"$_cl\" ]\n+}\n+rest_raw() { # rest_raw <user:pass> <path> <json> <timeout-s>  -> \"<status> <body>\"\n+    _f=\"/tmp/chr-rest.$$\"; rm -f \"$_f\" \"$_f.out\"; mkfifo \"$_f\" || return 1\n+    timeout \"$(( $4 + 5 ))\" nc \"$VM_EP\" 80 <\"$_f\" >\"$_f.out\" 2>/dev/null &\n+    _pid=$!\n+    exec 7>\"$_f\"\n+    printf 'POST /rest/%s HTTP/1.1\\r\\nHost: [%s]\\r\\nAuthorization: Basic %s\\r\\nContent-Type: application/json\\r\\nContent-Length: %s\\r\\nConnection: close\\r\\n\\r\\n%s' \\\n+        \"$2\" \"$VM_LL\" \"$(printf '%s' \"$1\" | base64 | tr -d '\\n')\" \\\n+        \"$(printf '%s' \"$3\" | wc -c | tr -d ' ')\" \"$3\" >&7\n+    _left=$4\n+    while kill -0 \"$_pid\" 2>/dev/null && ! resp_complete \"$_f.out\" && [ \"$_left\" -gt 0 ]; do\n+        sleep 1; _left=$(( _left - 1 ))\n+    done\n+    exec 7>&-\n+    wait \"$_pid\" 2>/dev/null\n+    _st=\"$(head -n1 \"$_f.out\" 2>/dev/null | tr -d '\\r' | cut -d' ' -f2)\"\n+    _body=\"$(sed '1,/^\\r*$/d' \"$_f.out\" 2>/dev/null | tr -d '\\r')\"\n+    rm -f \"$_f\" \"$_f.out\"\n+    printf '%s %s' \"${_st:-000}\" \"$_body\"\n+}\n+# RouterOS REST returns the object/array on success and an empty body for a\n+# bare `set`: only an explicit error object is a failure here. A silently\n+# failed call is caught by the read-back checks that follow each step.\n+is_err() { case \"$1\" in *'\"error\"'*) return 0 ;; *) return 1 ;; esac; }\n+\n+# ---- wait for RouterOS ----------------------------------------------------\n+waited=0\n+AUTH=\"\"\n+# A fresh official CHR disk has ONLY the factory `admin` account (blank\n+# password): the first boot authenticates as that account whatever --username\n+# asks for, and the requested user is created below. On a restart the pair that\n+# answers is the requested user.\n+while [ -z \"$AUTH\" ]; do\n+    for _cred in \"admin:\" \"admin:$PASSWORD\" \"$USERNAME:$PASSWORD\"; do\n+        rest \"$_cred\" system/resource >/dev/null 2>&1 && { AUTH=\"$_cred\"; break; }\n+    done\n+    [ -n \"$AUTH\" ] && break\n+    if ! kill -0 \"$QEMU_PID\" 2>/dev/null; then\n+        log \"qemu exited during boot\"\n+        exit 1\n+    fi\n+    sleep 2\n+    waited=$((waited + 2))\n+    if [ \"$waited\" -ge \"$BOOT_WAIT\" ]; then\n+        log \"RouterOS did not answer within ${BOOT_WAIT}s: leaving qemu running\"\n+        wait \"$QEMU_PID\"\n+        exit 1\n+    fi\n+done\n+log \"RouterOS answered after ${waited}s\"\n+\n+# Ready means a user can log in: RouterOS starts its SSH server well after REST\n+# answers on a slow boot (about 40 s later under TCG on arm64), so when the\n+# configuration keeps SSH enabled, its port must send the banner first.\n+# A config that disables SSH is not waited for; a banner that never comes is\n+# logged and the node is still declared ready (REST works).\n+ssh_banner() { (sleep 3) | nc \"$VM_EP\" 22 2>/dev/null | head -c 7; }\n+ready() {\n+    case \"$(rest \"$AUTH\" \"ip/service?name=ssh\")\" in\n+        *'\"disabled\":\"false\"'*)\n+            _t0=$(date +%s)\n+            until [ \"$(ssh_banner)\" = \"SSH-2.0\" ]; do\n+                if [ $(( $(date +%s) - _t0 )) -ge \"$BOOT_WAIT\" ]; then\n+                    log \"SSH did not answer within $(( $(date +%s) - _t0 ))s (REST works)\"\n+                    break\n+                fi\n+                sleep 2\n+            done\n+            ;;\n+    esac\n+    touch \"$READY_FILE\" 2>/dev/null || true\n+    log \"RouterOS ready\"\n+}\n+\n+# A failed step WITHHOLDS the ready marker (qemu keeps running, so the node can\n+# still be inspected over the serial console, port 5000 in the container).\n+bootstrap_fail() {\n+    log \"credential bootstrap FAILED: $1\"\n+    wait \"$QEMU_PID\"\n+    exit 0\n+}\n+\n+# ---- management: ether1 addressing and its own routing table ----------------\n+# ether1 gets the container's management addresses, and their return path lives\n+# in a `mgmt` routing table selected by a rule on those source addresses: a\n+# default route the lab adds to `main` (a static 0.0.0.0/0, a PPPoE client's\n+# default route, a dual-WAN pair) would otherwise take SSH/REST replies away\n+# from the management network while the data plane works. CHR's default ether1\n+# DHCP client is disabled: the address is static. Everything is tagged\n+# clab-mgmt and re-applied on every boot from the container's CURRENT addressing\n+# (a restart can bring a new IP), while the bootstrap channel itself\n+# (link-local) never depends on it.\n+mgmt_fail() {\n+    log \"management setup FAILED: $1 (redeploy the node: containerlab deploy --reconfigure)\"\n+    wait \"$QEMU_PID\"\n+    exit 0\n+}\n+rest_has() { # rest_has <path?query>: the GET answered a non-empty JSON array\n+    case \"$(rest \"$AUTH\" \"$1\")\" in '['*'{'*) return 0 ;; *) return 1 ;; esac\n+}\n+rest_add() { # rest_add <what> <path> <json>: a 2xx with no error object, else mgmt_fail with the router's detail\n+    _r=\"$(rest_raw \"$AUTH\" \"$2\" \"$3\" 20)\"\n+    case \"$_r\" in\n+        2??\\ *) is_err \"$_r\" && mgmt_fail \"$1: ${_r#* }\" ;;\n+        *) mgmt_fail \"$1: ${_r:-no response}\" ;;\n+    esac\n+}\n+rest_count() { # rest_count <path?query>: number of objects the GET answered\n+    rest \"$AUTH\" \"$1\" | tr '{' '\\n' | grep -c '\"\\.id\"'\n+}\n+rest_clear() { # rest_clear <menu>: remove its clab-mgmt objects\n+    _ids=\"$(rest \"$AUTH\" \"$1?comment=$MGMT_COMMENT\" | tr '{' '\\n' \\\n+        | sed -n 's/.*\"\\.id\":\"\\([^\"]*\\)\".*/\\1/p' | paste -sd, -)\"\n+    [ -z \"$_ids\" ] || rest_add \"remove old $1\" \"$1/remove\" \"{\\\".id\\\":\\\"$_ids\\\"}\"\n+}\n+# The tagged objects already match the container's addressing (a restart with\n+# the same IP): leave them alone, re-applying would only drop live sessions.\n+mgmt_in_place() {\n+    _want=0\n+    if [ -n \"$M4\" ]; then\n+        rest_has \"ip/address?interface=ether1&address=$M4&comment=$MGMT_COMMENT\" || return 1\n+        rest_has \"routing/rule?src-address=${M4%/*}/32&table=$MGMT_TABLE\" || return 1\n+        [ -z \"$G4\" ] || rest_has \"ip/route?routing-table=$MGMT_TABLE&dst-address=0.0.0.0/0&gateway=$G4\" || return 1\n+        _want=$((_want + 1))\n+    fi\n+    if [ -n \"$M6\" ]; then\n+        rest_has \"ipv6/address?interface=ether1&address=$M6&comment=$MGMT_COMMENT\" || return 1\n+        _want=$((_want + 1))\n+    fi\n+    [ \"$(rest_count \"ip/address?comment=$MGMT_COMMENT\")$(rest_count \"ipv6/address?comment=$MGMT_COMMENT\")\" \\\n+        = \"$([ -n \"$M4\" ] && echo 1 || echo 0)$([ -n \"$M6\" ] && echo 1 || echo 0)\" ] || return 1\n+    rest_has \"ipv6/nd?interface=ether1&ra-lifetime=none\" || return 1\n+    [ \"$_want\" -gt 0 ]\n+}\n+mgmt_setup() {\n+    if mgmt_in_place; then\n+        log \"management on ether1 unchanged: ${M4:-no IPv4}${M6:+, $M6}\"\n+        return 0\n+    fi\n+    for _m in routing/rule ip/route ipv6/route ip/address ipv6/address; do rest_clear \"$_m\"; done\n+    rest_has \"routing/table?name=$MGMT_TABLE\" \\\n+        || rest_add \"routing table $MGMT_TABLE\" routing/table/add \\\n+            \"{\\\"name\\\":\\\"$MGMT_TABLE\\\",\\\"fib\\\":\\\"yes\\\",\\\"comment\\\":\\\"$MGMT_COMMENT\\\"}\"\n+    _c=\"\\\"comment\\\":\\\"$MGMT_COMMENT\\\"\"\n+    if [ -n \"$M4\" ]; then\n+        _net4=\"$(ipcalc -n \"$M4\" | sed -n 's/^NETWORK=//p')/${M4#*/}\"\n+        rest_add \"ether1 address $M4\" ip/address/add \"{\\\"address\\\":\\\"$M4\\\",\\\"interface\\\":\\\"ether1\\\",$_c}\"\n+        rest_add \"route $_net4 in table $MGMT_TABLE\" ip/route/add \\\n+            \"{\\\"dst-address\\\":\\\"$_net4\\\",\\\"gateway\\\":\\\"ether1\\\",\\\"routing-table\\\":\\\"$MGMT_TABLE\\\",$_c}\"\n+        [ -z \"$G4\" ] || rest_add \"default via $G4 in table $MGMT_TABLE\" ip/route/add \\\n+            \"{\\\"dst-address\\\":\\\"0.0.0.0/0\\\",\\\"gateway\\\":\\\"$G4\\\",\\\"routing-table\\\":\\\"$MGMT_TABLE\\\",$_c}\"\n+        rest_add \"routing rule for ${M4%/*}\" routing/rule/add \\\n+            \"{\\\"src-address\\\":\\\"${M4%/*}/32\\\",\\\"action\\\":\\\"lookup-only-in-table\\\",\\\"table\\\":\\\"$MGMT_TABLE\\\",$_c}\"\n+    fi\n+    if [ -n \"$M6\" ]; then\n+        rest_add \"ether1 address $M6\" ipv6/address/add \\\n+            \"{\\\"address\\\":\\\"$M6\\\",\\\"interface\\\":\\\"ether1\\\",\\\"advertise\\\":\\\"no\\\",$_c}\"\n+        # RouterOS normalizes the host bits of dst-address (::3/64 -> ::/64)\n+        rest_add \"IPv6 route $M6 in table $MGMT_TABLE\" ipv6/route/add \\\n+            \"{\\\"dst-address\\\":\\\"$M6\\\",\\\"gateway\\\":\\\"ether1\\\",\\\"routing-table\\\":\\\"$MGMT_TABLE\\\",$_c}\"\n+        [ -z \"$G6\" ] || rest_add \"IPv6 default via $G6 in table $MGMT_TABLE\" ipv6/route/add \\\n+            \"{\\\"dst-address\\\":\\\"::/0\\\",\\\"gateway\\\":\\\"$G6%ether1\\\",\\\"routing-table\\\":\\\"$MGMT_TABLE\\\",$_c}\"\n+        rest_add \"routing rule for ${M6%/*}\" routing/rule/add \\\n+            \"{\\\"src-address\\\":\\\"${M6%/*}/128\\\",\\\"action\\\":\\\"lookup-only-in-table\\\",\\\"table\\\":\\\"$MGMT_TABLE\\\",$_c}\"\n+    fi\n+    _id=\"$(rest \"$AUTH\" \"ip/dhcp-client?interface=ether1\" | sed -n 's/.*\"\\.id\":\"\\([^\"]*\\)\".*/\\1/p' | head -n1)\"\n+    [ -z \"$_id\" ] || rest_add \"disable the ether1 DHCP client\" ip/dhcp-client/set \\\n+        \"{\\\"numbers\\\":\\\"$_id\\\",\\\"disabled\\\":\\\"yes\\\"}\"\n+    # RouterOS's default ND entry (interface=all, ra-lifetime=30m) sends router\n+    # advertisements on ether1 too, and every host on the management network\n+    # with accept_ra would install the router as its IPv6 default route. An\n+    # ether1 entry with ra-lifetime=none keeps the RAs harmless and withdraws a\n+    # route a host already learned (a disabled entry does not override \"all\").\n+    # /ipv6/nd keeps no comment, so its entry is found by interface.\n+    _nd=\"$(rest \"$AUTH\" \"ipv6/nd?interface=ether1\" | sed -n 's/.*\"\\.id\":\"\\([^\"]*\\)\".*/\\1/p' | head -n1)\"\n+    if [ -n \"$_nd\" ]; then\n+        rest_add \"ether1 router advertisements off\" ipv6/nd/set \\\n+            \"{\\\"numbers\\\":\\\"$_nd\\\",\\\"ra-lifetime\\\":\\\"none\\\",\\\"disabled\\\":\\\"no\\\"}\"\n+    else\n+        rest_add \"ether1 router advertisements off\" ipv6/nd/add \\\n+            \"{\\\"interface\\\":\\\"ether1\\\",\\\"ra-lifetime\\\":\\\"none\\\"}\"\n+    fi\n+    # Prove they took (a transport flake answers an empty body, which is not\n+    # an error by the set-contract).\n+    if [ -n \"$M4\" ]; then\n+        rest_has \"ip/address?interface=ether1&address=$M4\" || mgmt_fail \"$M4 not on ether1\"\n+        rest_has \"routing/rule?src-address=${M4%/*}/32&table=$MGMT_TABLE\" \\\n+            || mgmt_fail \"routing rule for ${M4%/*} not applied\"\n+    fi\n+    log \"management on ether1: ${M4:-no IPv4}${M6:+, $M6}, return path in routing table $MGMT_TABLE\"\n+}\n+# Join the management network, then have the router reach its gateway from the\n+# management address: proves the return path both ways, and the ARP/ND request\n+# refreshes the gateway's neighbor entry when the IP was used by another MAC\n+# before (a redeployed lab hands out the same addresses).\n+mgmt_attach() {\n+    [ -e \"$SYSNET/$MGMT_BR/brif/eth0\" ] || brctl addif \"$MGMT_BR\" eth0\n+    ip link set eth0 up\n+    for _pair in \"$G4 ${M4%/*}\" \"$G6 ${M6%/*}\"; 1235: +// filterLauncherConfig drops what the launcher configures at every boot from an /export:
1236: +// every object tagged clab-mgmt (the ether1 management addresses, the mgmt routing table,
1237: +// its routes and rules), the ether1 ND entry and CHR's ether1 DHCP client. Imported again
1238: +// as a startup config, these lines duplicate objects the launcher already created and
1239: +// RouterOS stops the import at the first one; the management addressing also belongs to
1240: +// the deployment, not the lab. Continuation lines (an unescaped trailing backslash) are joined
1241: +// before
1242: +// matching.
1243: +func filterLauncherConfig(config string) string {
1244: +	var out []string
1245: +
1246: +	section := ""
1247: +	pending := ""
1248: +	for line := range strings.SplitSeq(strings.ReplaceAll(config, "\r\n", "\n"), "\n") {
1249: +		if pending != "" {
1250: +			line = pending + "\n" + line
1251: +			pending = ""
1252: +		}
1253: +		if continues(line) {
1254: +			pending = line
1255: +			continue
1256: +		}
1257: +
1258: +		trimmed := strings.TrimSpace(line)
1259: +		if strings.HasPrefix(trimmed, "/") {
1260: +			section = trimmed
1261: +		}
1262: +		if launcherOwned(section, trimmed) {
1263: +			continue
1264: +		}
1265: +		out = append(out, line)
1266: +	}
1267: +	if pending != "" {
1268: +		out = append(out, pending)
1269: +	}
1270: +
1271: +	return strings.Join(out, "\n")
1272: +}
1273: +
1274: +// continues reports whether an /export line continues on the next one: it ends in an
1275: +// unescaped backslash. RouterOS writes a literal backslash in a value as `\\`, so an even
1276: +// run of trailing backslashes ends the line.
1277: +func continues(line string) bool {
1278: +	trailing := len(line) - len(strings.TrimRight(line, `\`))
1279: +
1280: +	return trailing%2 == 1
1281: +}
1282: +
1283: +func launcherOwned(section, line string) bool {
1284: +	if !strings.HasPrefix(line, "add ") {
1285: +		return false
1286: +	}
1287: +
1288: +	fields := strings.Fields(strings.ReplaceAll(line, `\`, " "))
1289: +	has := func(kv string) bool { return slices.Contains(fields, kv) }
1290: +
1291: +	if has("comment=" + mgmtComment) {
1292: +		return true
1293: +	}
1294: +
1295: +	// /ipv6 nd keeps no comment: the launcher's ether1 entry is matched by interface.
1296: +	switch section {
1297: +	case "/ip address", "/ipv6 address", "/ip dhcp-client", "/ipv6 nd":
1298: +		return has("interface=ether1")
1299: +	}
1300: +
1301: +	return false
1302: +}

1371: +	if n.Cfg.Env["USERNAME"] != "netops" || n.Cfg.Env["PASSWORD"] != "pass word#1" {
1372: +		t.Errorf("Env credentials = %q/%q, want netops/%q",
1373: +			n.Cfg.Env["USERNAME"], n.Cfg.Env["PASSWORD"], "pass word#1")
1374: +	}
1375: +
1376: +	for _, want := range []string{
1377: +		filepath.Join(labDir, "ftpboot") + ":/ftpboot",
1378: +		filepath.Join(labDir, "chr-launch.sh") + ":/chr-launch.sh:ro",
1379: +	} {
1380: +		if !slices.Contains(n.Cfg.Binds, want) {
1381: +			t.Errorf("Binds = %v, want it to contain %q", n.Cfg.Binds, want)
1382: +		}
1383: +	}
1384: +
1385: +	hc := n.Cfg.Healthcheck
1386: +	if hc == nil || !slices.Equal(hc.Test, []string{"CMD-SHELL", "test -f /.chr-launch-ready"}) {
1387: +		t.Errorf("Healthcheck = %+v, want the launcher's ready file check", hc)
1388: +	}
1389: +
1390: +	if n.HostRequirements.VirtRequired {
1391: +		t.Error("VirtRequired = true, want false: the image falls back to TCG without KVM")
1392: +	}
1393: +}

1395: +func TestInitKeepsUserSettings(t *testing.T) {
1396: +	userHC := &clabtypes.HealthcheckConfig{Test: []string{"CMD", "true"}}
1397: +	n := newInitialized(t, &clabtypes.NodeConfig{
1398: +		ShortName:   "r1",
1399: +		LabDir:      t.TempDir(),
1400: +		Entrypoint:  "/start.sh",
1401: +		Healthcheck: userHC,
1402: +		Env:         map[string]string{"MEM": "1024", "PASSWORD": "override"},
1403: +	})
1404: +
1405: +	if n.Cfg.Entrypoint != "/start.sh" {
1406: +		t.Errorf("Entrypoint = %q, want the user's", n.Cfg.Entrypoint)
1407: +	}
1408: +	if n.Cfg.Healthcheck != userHC {
1409: +		t.Errorf("Healthcheck = %+v, want the user's", n.Cfg.Healthcheck)
1410: +	}
1411: +	if n.Cfg.Env["MEM"] != "1024" || n.Cfg.Env["PASSWORD"] != "override" {
1412: +		t.Errorf("Env = %v, want the user's MEM and PASSWORD kept", n.Cfg.Env)
1413: +	}
1414: +}

1416: +func TestPreDeployWritesLauncherAndStartupConfig(t *testing.T) {
1417: +	labDir := t.TempDir()
1418: +	startup := filepath.Join(t.TempDir(), "r1.rsc")
1419: +	rsc := "/ip address add address=10.0.0.1/30 interface=ether2\n"
1420: +	if err := os.WriteFile(startup, []byte(rsc), 0o600); err != nil {
1421: +		t.Fatal(err)
1422: +	}
1423: +
1424: +	n := newInitialized(t, &clabtypes.NodeConfig{
1425: +		ShortName:     "r1",
1426: +		LongName:      "clab-test-r1",
1427: +		LabDir:        labDir,
1428: +		StartupConfig: startup,
1429: +	})
1430: +
1431: +	if err := n.PreDeploy(context.Background(), &clabnodes.PreDeployParams{}); err != nil {
1432: +		t.Fatalf("PreDeploy failed: %v", err)
1433: +	}
1434: +
1435: +	got, err := os.ReadFile(filepath.Join(labDir, "chr-launch.sh"))
1436: +	if err != nil {
1437: +		t.Fatalf("launcher not written: %v", err)
1438: +	}
1439: +	if !bytes.Equal(got, launcherScript) {
1440: +		t.Error("written launcher differs from the embedded chr-launch.sh")
1441: +	}
1442: +	if !bytes.HasPrefix(got, []byte("#!/bin/sh\n")) {
1443: +		t.Error("embedded launcher does not start with #!/bin/sh")
1444: +	}
1445: +
1446: +	cfg, err := os.ReadFile(filepath.Join(labDir, "ftpboot", "config.auto.rsc"))
1447: +	if err != nil {
1448: +		t.Fatalf("startup config not written: %v", err)
1449: +	}
1450: +	if string(cfg) != rsc {
1451: +		t.Errorf("startup config = %q, want %q", cfg, rsc)
1452: +	}
1453: +}
